DatasourceVariable that --source-map does not namedatasource that --source-map does not nameunsupportednoneHune has no data source variable (open question D10). Fallback: the dashboard is not imported.
AdhocVariableadhocchangedkind = adhoc (4.10c)The source comes from the source map and MUST be a ClickHouse source; else unsupported, fallback: the variable is removed. The keys are the static keys of defaultKeys (value, else text), then the keys of filters and of baseFilters, each one once. A key that is not a plain column name, a key that the list adhoc_columns of the source (with -c) does not hold, and a key after the 64th are unsupported; fallback: the key is removed. With no key left, the keys are the first 64 items of the list (changed). With no list, the variable is unsupported; fallback: the variable is removed. A stored filter and a base filter do not apply (changed). allowCustomValue is metadata. The variable has one more changed item: the filters apply only where a query has AND $__adhocFilters. Each panel query and extra query on the source of the variable gets a changed item that asks for AND $__adhocFilters in its WHERE clause. The importer never changes the SQL.
GroupByVariablegroupbyunsupportednoneNot in the measured set. Fallback: the dashboard is not imported.
IntervalVariableintervalmappedkind = interval (4.10a)See the rows below.
ConstantVariableconstantmappedkind = constant (4.10b)See the rows below.
query of an interval variablequerymappedintervalsThe importer splits the text at each run of characters that are not a letter, a digit or _, as Grafana does (measured). It removes a repeated item and keeps the order. An item that breaks 4.10a (abc, 5x, 0m, 1M) is unsupported; fallback: the item is removed and listed. With no item left, the variable is unsupported; fallback: the dashboard is not imported. An empty text gives the default list of Grafana (1m, 10m, 30m, 1h, 6h, 12h, 1d, 7d, 14d, 30d), changed. More than 50 items give unsupported; fallback: the first 50 items. A value that is not a text is unsupported; fallback: the dashboard is not imported.
auto, auto_count, auto_min of an interval variableauto, auto_count, auto_minmappedsameAn absent auto gives false. An absent auto_count gives 30 and an absent auto_min gives 10s, the defaults of Grafana, with no item. An auto_count from 1 to 1,000 is kept, else unsupported with the fallback 30. An auto_min that is a table text of 7.4a is kept. Another valid interval text gives the largest table text that is not above it (changed; 90s gives 1m, as Grafana does). A text of more than 365 days gives 1y (changed). An invalid text gives 10s (changed).
current of an interval variablecurrentmappeddefault$__auto and $__auto_interval_<name> give $__auto when auto is true, else null (changed). An item gives that item. Another value gives null (changed).
query of a constant variablequerymappedvalueA value of more than 1,024 bytes, or with a control character, is unsupported; fallback: the dashboard is not imported. An absent value gives an empty text.
hide of a constant variablehidechangedhide = variableGrafana shows a constant with hide 0, and Hune never shows it. The row is listed when hide is 0 or 1.
description, skipUrlSync, options and current of a constant variablesamedroppednoneMetadata. Listed when not empty.
namenamemappedname (4.3)A name outside the rule of 4.3 (for example one that starts with __) is unsupported; fallback: the dashboard is not imported.
labellabelmappedlabel (4.7)Empty gives null.
descriptiondescriptiondroppednoneFormat 1 has no description of a variable (4.7). Listed when not empty.
hidehidemappedhide (4.7)dontHide or 0 gives none. hideLabel or 1 gives label. hideVariable or 2 gives variable.
query of a query variablequery or definitionmappedsql (4.16) and source (4.8)Same rules as a panel query.
multi, includeAll, allValuesamemappedmulti, include_all, all_value (4.8, 4.9)An empty allValue, or any allValue with includeAll = false, gives null.
allowCustomValue of a QueryVariable (v2beta1: spec.variables[].spec.allowCustomValue; v1: templating.list[].allowCustomValue)samemappedallow_custom_value (4.8)true and an absent member both give true: Grafana 12.4.2 reads an absent member as true. false gives no member (4.38). A value that is not a boolean is unsupported (19.18); fallback: no member.
allowCustomValue of a CustomVariable (same paths)samemappedallow_custom_value (4.9)true gives true. false gives no member (4.38). An absent member gives no member too, with the class changed and one report line: Grafana reads it as true, and Hune keeps the variable closed, so the server keeps its option check. The member stays absent, with the class changed and one report line, when a free value would be refused: a placeholder of the variable in a quoted name, a placeholder in code with the format none, raw or csv, or (MongoDB) an option value that starts with $ and a query that uses the variable. A query uses the variable when a placeholder of 7.6 names it. Each form counts: $name, ${name}, ${name:format} and [[name]], which the importer rewrites to ${name}. The importer reads the placeholders with the scan of 7.6, so $names names another variable. A value that is not a boolean is unsupported (19.18); fallback: no member.
allowCustomValue of a TextVariablesamedroppednoneMetadata. A text variable is always free (4.10). Not listed. The v2beta1 fixture monitor-v2b holds it with the value true.
refresh = onDashboardLoad or 1samemappedrefresh = load (4.8)
refresh = onTimeRangeChanged or 2samemappedrefresh = time_change
refresh = never or 0samechangedrefresh = loadHune computes the options at load. Grafana keeps stored options.
sortsortmappedsort (4.8)v1 number, then v2 name: 0 or disabled gives none; 1 or alphabeticalAsc gives alpha_asc; 2 or alphabeticalDesc gives alpha_desc; 3 or numericalAsc gives num_asc; 4 or numericalDesc gives num_desc; 5 or alphabeticalCaseInsensitiveAsc gives alpha_ci_asc; 6 or alphabeticalCaseInsensitiveDesc gives alpha_ci_desc; 7 or naturalAsc gives natural_asc; 8 or naturalDesc gives natural_desc. A value that 4.8 does not hold is unsupported (19.18); fallback: none.
regexregexmappedregex (4.8, Grafana form of 4.24)A pattern or a flag that 4.24 refuses is unsupported (19.18); fallback: null.
currentcurrentmappeddefault (4.11)All gives $__all. A value of a custom variable that is not an option value is unsupported when the mapping makes the variable closed; fallback: an empty array. When the variable is open, the value is a default item (4.11, rule 5).
options of a query variablesamedroppednoneMetadata. Hune computes the options.
skipUrlSyncsamedroppednoneListed when true.

Folders and objects outside the dashboards:

ObjectClassHuneReason or fallback
folder with a uid that fits the folder id rule of 4.3mappedfolder id and the title of its folder file (4.41)15.7.
root of Grafanamappedno folder (4.3)The dashboards of the root have no folder.
folder with a parent (parentUid)unsupportednoneFolders have one level. Fallback: its dashboards are not imported.
version history (138 versions measured)not importednoneNot in the export. The report header says so.
public dashboard (1 measured)not importednonePublic dashboards are removed in v1 (section 18).
short links (7 measured)not importednoneThe nginx map of 15.17 redirects them.
permissions, stars, playlists, snapshots, alert rulesnot importednoneNot in the export, and out of scope.

15.7 The folder map

The file of --folders is JSON with a closed schema:

json
{
  "folders": [
    { "grafana_uid": "fe1x0abc2kq9", "id": "transit", "title": "Transit desk" },
    { "grafana_uid": "ae7r4kd0pq1z", "id": "nodes" }
  ]
}
MemberTypeRule
grafana_uidtextNecessary. A folder uid of folders.json. Unique in the file.
idtextNecessary. The folder id rule of 4.3.
titletextOptional. 1 to 256 characters (4.41). When absent, the importer takes the Grafana title.

A folder without an entry gets its Grafana uid as its id when the uid fits the rule. Otherwise its dashboards are unsupported with the fallback "the dashboard is not imported". Two Grafana folders that map to one id stop the command with exit code 2. A file that breaks the schema stops the command with exit code 2.

15.8 The uid rule

The importer keeps a Grafana uid that fits the uid rule of 4.3. Otherwise it makes a new uid with these steps:

  1. Change ASCII capital letters to small letters.
  2. Change each character outside [a-z0-9-] to -.
  3. Change each run of - to one -. Remove - at the start and at the end. Keep the first 40 characters.
  4. Check the result. When it is empty, breaks the rule or equals the uid of another imported dashboard, replace it. The replacement is g- and the first 16 hex characters of the SHA-256 of the old uid.

Example: Nodes_Prod becomes nodes-prod. The new uid is an item of class changed with the old and the new uid. The nginx map of 15.17 redirects the old uid to the new uid. The steps give the same result at each run.

15.9 The source map

Each --source-map gives a Grafana data source uid and a source id of section 8. Example: --source-map clickhouse=prod maps the Grafana data source with the uid clickhouse to the source prod.

The importer reads the data source of a query here: datasource.uid in v1, PanelQuery.spec.datasource.uid in v2alpha1, and DataQuery.datasource.name in v2beta1. An old v1 reference that is a text (a data source name) is compared with the keys of --source-map too.

A key can also name a data source variable or an item of __inputs with type = datasource, for example --source-map datasource=prod. Hune has no data source variable (open question D10). Thus the importer replaces each reference $name, ${name} and [[name]] in a member datasource with the source id. This applies to the panels, the targets, the query variables and the annotations.

The variable and the item of __inputs leave the document, each with one item of class changed. The text of the item of a variable gives its regex, multi and includeAll. A reference with no type takes the plugin of the variable (query or pluginId) or of the item (pluginId) as its type.

A reference that the map does not name is unsupported; fallback: panel removed. Its reason is "the data source is a variable that the source map does not name". A data source variable that the map does not name is unsupported; fallback: the dashboard is not imported. A SQL text that names such a variable or item ($datasource in the SQL) is unsupported, because the importer cannot give it a value. Fallback: panel removed, item removed for an annotation, or the dashboard is not imported for a query variable.

The source id must fit the label rule of a source block (13.12, 13.12a, 13.12b). The kind of the block follows the type of the Grafana data source. The type grafana-clickhouse-datasource gives a clickhouse block. The types grafana-postgresql-datasource and postgres give a postgres block.

The type haohanyang-mongodb-datasource gives a mongodb block. With -c, the id must name a block of that kind, else exit code 2. Without -c, the importer takes the dialect of 7.19 from the type.

The importer checks the kind of the block after the conversion. This check runs for each source id that a panel or a variable uses with a typed data source. A block of another kind stops the command with exit code 2, before any write. A reference with no type, such as an old data source name, takes the kind of the block. Without -c, such a reference has the dialect clickhouse. With -c, each source id of the map MUST name a block of one of the three kinds, else exit code 2.

15.10 The acceptance file

The file of --accept lists the losses that the operator accepts. A person with the right to accept the losses signs the file. The file stays with the confidential exports and never enters the repository (15.18).

text
# Losses accepted for the import of 2026-11-20. Signed: the operator.
abc123:/spec/elements/panel-7/spec/vizConfig/spec/options/legend/sortBy   # legend sort: not used
k9x2mm:/panels/3/targets/1                                                 # second query: a copy of the first

The rules:

  • One item id on each line. A # after a space starts a comment. Blank lines and comment lines are permitted.
  • An item id is <grafana uid>:<JSON pointer>. The JSON pointer (RFC 6901) names the member in the input file of that dashboard. An item of folders.json uses folder:<folder uid>:<JSON pointer>.
  • A duplicate item id stops the command with exit code 2.
  • An item id that matches no item of the run is stale. A stale line gives exit code 1, the same as an item without acceptance. So the file always describes the current export.

15.11 The report

--report <path> writes <path>.json for programs and <path>.md for the operator. The two files hold the same facts. Dashboards are in the order of their Grafana uid, and items in the order of their JSON pointer.

json
{
  "report_version": 1,
  "hune_version": "0.2.0",
  "created_at": "2026-11-20T09:12:44Z",
  "mode": "dry_run",
  "input": {
    "dir": "/srv/grafana-export-20261120T090000Z",
    "grafana_version": "12.2.1",
    "clickhouse_plugin_version": "4.5.1",
    "files": [ { "name": "dashboards/abc123.json", "sha256": "9f2c...", "form": "v2beta1" } ],
    "ignored_files": []
  },
  "options": {
    "source_map": { "clickhouse": "prod" },
    "link_prefix": { "old": "/grafana/", "new": "/dashboards/" },
    "folders_file": "folders.map.json",
    "accept_file": "accept.txt",
    "write": false,
    "replace": false
  },
  "totals": {
    "dashboards": 10, "panels": 161,
    "mapped": 4210, "changed": 12, "dropped": 4, "unsupported": 0, "metadata": 380,
    "accepted": 16, "unaccepted": 0, "stale_accept_lines": 0
  },
  "not_imported": { "history": "not in the export", "public_dashboards": 1, "short_links": 7 },
  "folders": [ { "grafana_uid": "fe1x0abc2kq9", "id": "transit", "title": "Transit desk", "result": "would_create" } ],
  "dashboards": [
    {
      "grafana_uid": "abc123", "uid": "abc123", "folder": "transit", "title": "Nodes",
      "panels": 45, "metadata": 41, "result": "would_write", "etag": "4be1...",
      "items": [
        {
          "id": "abc123:/spec/elements/panel-7/spec/vizConfig/spec/options/legend/sortBy",
          "class": "unsupported", "panel_id": 7, "panel_title": "Score by day",
          "member": "options.legend.sortBy",
          "reason": "not in the option set of the panel type",
          "fallback": "member removed",
          "value": "\"Max\"", "truncated": false, "accepted": true
        }
      ]
    }
  ],
  "redirects": [
    { "from": "/grafana/d/abc123", "to": "/dashboards/d/abc123" },
    { "from": "/grafana/goto/xYz12AB", "to": "/dashboards/d/abc123/nodes?from=now-7d&to=now" }
  ],
  "exit_code": 0
}
MemberRule
modedry_run or write.
etagThe etag of section 4 of the document that the importer makes.
valueThe original value as compact JSON, cut to 200 characters at a character boundary. truncated tells if it was cut. An unsupported item of a MongoDB query gives the JSON of its member, cut by the same rule.
panel_id, panel_titlenull for a member outside a panel.
folders[].resultexists, would_create, created, would_rename, renamed, refused_title (15.13).
dashboards[].resultOne value of the table below.
ResultMeaning
would_writeDry run: the document is new, or differs from the store in its bytes or its folder.
would_refuseDry run with -c: the uid exists with other bytes or in another folder, and --replace is absent.
unchangedThe store holds the same bytes in the same folder. No write, no history version.
writtenThe importer created the document.
replacedThe importer saved a new version over other bytes (--replace).
movedThe importer moved the document to its new folder; the bytes were equal (--replace).
moved_replacedThe importer moved the document, then saved a new version over other bytes (--replace).
refused_existsThe uid exists with other bytes or in another folder and --replace is absent, or the uid is deleted.
blockedAn item needs acceptance and has none.
not_importedAn accepted item has the fallback "the dashboard is not imported".
store_errorThe store refused or failed the write.

The Markdown report has these parts, in this order:

  1. The input directory, the Grafana version and the plugin version.
  2. The totals, as a table.
  3. The objects that are not imported.
  4. One table of items for each dashboard: id, class, panel, member, reason, fallback, accepted.
  5. The redirects, as an nginx block.
  6. The exit code and its reason.

15.12 Exit codes

CodeMeaningDocuments written
0Each item that needs acceptance is accepted, no line is stale, and each write succeeded.yes with --write
1An item needs acceptance and has none, or an acceptance line is stale.none
2Usage or input error: an option, a file, a schema version, a map file, the configuration.none
3Write refused or failed: a uid with other bytes or in another folder, or a folder with another title, without --replace; a deleted uid; a held volume lock; a store error.15.13

15.13 The write and the idempotent final import

With --write, the importer does these steps in this order:

  1. Read and convert every input file. Check the acceptance file. On exit code 1 or 2, stop here.
  2. Open the store of -c. For the volume, take the writer lock of 5.16. A held lock gives exit code 3.

  3. Read the folder, its title, and the etag of each target uid. Without --replace, stop with exit code 3 for each of these cases:

    Also stop with exit code 3 when a uid is deleted, with or without --replace. Nothing is written yet.

    • a uid has other bytes;
    • a uid is in another folder;
    • a folder has another title.
  4. Create the folders that do not exist. With --replace, call Store::rename_folder for each folder whose title differs (folder result renamed).
  5. Write each dashboard in the order of its Grafana uid, by the rules below.
State in the storeActionResult
uid absent, with no versionStore::create with the kind importwritten
same bytes, same foldernoneunchanged
other bytes, same folder, --replaceStore::save with the kind import and If-Match equal to the stored etagreplaced
same bytes, other folder, --replaceStore::move_to to the new folder, with If-Match equal to the stored etagmoved
other bytes, other folder, --replaceStore::move_to, then Store::save with the kind importmoved_replaced
uid deleted (versions only)none; step 3 stops with exit code 3refused_exists

Only hune restore (5.40) restores a deleted uid, because create gives Exists for a uid with versions (5.4). A current file that is not valid (4.39, rule 3) has the etag of its raw bytes. A save over it with that If-Match first records a version of kind external without bytes (5.14), then the version of kind import.

Each write records a history version of the kind import (5.2), or of the kind move for a move. Its saved_by is import-grafana, and its message is import-grafana <file> <sha12>. <sha12> is the first 12 hex characters of the SHA-256 of the input file.

The save_id of each write is a UUID version 8 (RFC 9562). Its 16 bytes are the first 16 bytes of a SHA-256, with the version and variant bits of RFC 9562 set. The input of the SHA-256 names one transition:

WriteInput of the SHA-256
createimport-grafana:<uid>:none:<new etag>
saveimport-grafana:<uid>:<If-Match>:<new etag>
moveimport-grafana:<uid>:<If-Match>:move:<new folder>

So each transition has its own save_id. Example: a dashboard changes from A to B, then to A again. The second A gets a new save_id, because its If-Match is the etag of B. A repeated write after a crash or a lost answer sends the same save_id. The store then answers replayed (5.5), and the result is the result of the first write: written, replaced, moved or moved_replaced.

A store error in step 5 stops the command with exit code 3. The dashboards before it stay written. A new run with the same input completes the work, because a written dashboard gives unchanged.

The same input and options give the same documents, byte for byte. The documents hold no time and no random value. A second run of the same command writes nothing and exits with code 0. The final import of the cutover (15.15) is the same command on the final export, with --write --replace.

A dry run with -c opens the store read-only and takes no lock. It reports would_write, would_refuse and unchanged for the dashboards, and would_create, would_rename and exists for the folders.

15.14 Verification on real dashboards

The verification proves that Hune shows the same data as Grafana. It runs on a trusted machine or on a private runner, never in a public CI. It reads the real Grafana and the real ClickHouse. So each run needs the authorisation of the person who is responsible for them.

It runs outside the peak hours. It sends only read queries through Grafana and through Hune. Its inputs and outputs stay outside the repository (15.18).

Each tool has one access method:

ToolAccessCredential
Export of 15.3SSH to the host of the Grafana instance, then docker exec in the Grafana containerThe SSH key of the operator
import-grafana and the test parity_privateNone: they read local files onlyNone
capture.mjs and compare.mjsHTTPS through the reverse proxy of the instance (nginx in this runbook): /grafana/ for Grafana and /dashboards/ for Hune (15.15)A login session of a person with access to both prefixes. This person logs in one time in the browser of the tool. The tool keeps the session state in the evidence directory (mode 0600), never the password, and removes it at the end of the run.

The tools need no change on the host of the instance: no SSH tunnel to the Grafana port and no new nginx location.

The verification has six steps. Each step has a pass criterion.

StepActionPass criterion
V1Run the importer in a dry run on a fresh export, with the signed acceptance file.Exit code 0; unaccepted = 0; no dashboard is blocked or not_imported.
V2Run --write two times into an empty temporary volume.The second run gives unchanged for each dashboard. The two JSON reports are equal except created_at. The volume tree is equal after each run.
V3Compare the SQL (15.14a).Each text is equal, or its difference has a cause that the operator signs.
V4Run the parity harness (15.14b).Each panel is equal, or its difference has a cause that the operator signs.
V5Visual check (15.14c).The operator signs the review.
V6Record the counts and the signatures in the evidence directory.The record exists and names the Grafana and plugin versions.

15.14a SQL parity

The capture tool is the Playwright script tools/parity/capture.mjs. It runs in the image tools/parity/Dockerfile, which starts from the pinned Playwright image. It opens each dashboard in Grafana at a fixed URL: absolute from and to, and the var- values of one set. The window is 1920 by 1080 pixels. The tool waits until no request is open.

The tool records each POST /grafana/api/ds/query request and its answer, for panels and for variables. It reads the dashboard uid and the panel id from the request headers X-Dashboard-Uid and X-Panel-Id.

A request without X-Panel-Id is a variable query. The compare program pairs it by three keys: the dashboard uid, the refId of the query, and its text. For the text key, the program expands the SQL of each query variable of that dashboard with the values of the set. It normalises the result by the rule below. The pair is the variable whose text equals the normalised rawSql of the request. A request that pairs with no variable or with two variables gets the cause other.

The time ranges are fixed and in the past. D is 00:00 UTC of the day of the run.

Rangefromto
R1D minus 1 hourD
R2D minus 24 hoursD
R3D minus 7 daysD

The variable sets:

SetValues
S1The saved values of each variable (current).
S2"All" for each variable with include_all; S1 for the others.
S3The first two options for each multi-value variable; S1 for the others.

So each dashboard opens 9 times: 3 ranges by 3 sets.

The compare program is the ignored test parity_private of the crate hune-grafana. The operator runs it with cargo test -p hune-grafana --test parity_private -- --ignored. It reads the capture directory from the variable HUNE_PARITY_DIR, because libtest reads each free argument as a test filter. For each recorded query, it expands the SQL of the imported document with the expander of section 7. The inputs of the expansion are the variable values, and from, to and maxDataPoints of the request. The min_interval of the panel is also an input.

The program compares the result with results.<refId>.frames[0].schema.meta.executedQueryString of the answer. It also compares the interval of section 7 with the intervalMs of the request. It normalises both texts first. The lexer of section 7 reads each text. Each run of white space outside literals and comments becomes one space. The program removes the white space at both ends.

A difference gets one cause:

CauseMeaningAction
escapeA value holds ' or \. Hune escapes it; Grafana inserts it raw.Expected by section 7. The operator signs the list.
intervalThe interval differs.Fix Hune, or the operator signs the cause.
formatA variable format gives other text.Fix Hune, or the operator signs the cause.
otherAny other difference.Fix Hune before the cutover.

The compare program also reads the panels of a PostgreSQL source. The Grafana PostgreSQL data source gives executedQueryString in the same member. The macros of 7.22 have the text of Grafana, so they give no difference. The causes escape and format apply, with the rules of 7.21. A macro of the dialect postgres in a string gives the cause other, and the report lists it as changed (15.6).

The compare program does not compare the query text of a MongoDB panel. The plugin gives no executed query in the form of 7.35, and the text that Hune sends is BSON. The check is by result: the disposable MongoDB of 16.9b holds synthetic data, and the harness runs the pipeline in the plugin and in Hune. It compares the frames with the types of 7.16c. The difference list is in tools/parity/vector-differences.json (16.13).

15.14b The parity harness

The harness is a second Playwright script, tools/parity/compare.mjs. It opens each dashboard in Grafana and in the Hune instance of the coexistence (15.15). Both get the same URL state and the same window. That Hune instance reads the imported documents and the same ClickHouse. The harness opens a panel in the two tools within one minute. Thus the data of a past range does not move.

For each panel, the harness compares these items:

  • For a table panel: the column headers in order, after the field configuration (display names, hidden columns).
  • For the other six types: the series names (the display names of 9.13).
  • For every panel: the data after the transformations, as display texts. Grafana gives them in "Inspect, Data", with the options "Data with transformations" and "Formatted data", as CSV. Hune gives them in the "View data" dialog of the panel (9.11). The harness reads the table of that dialog. The button "Download CSV" of the dialog gives the same data as a file (9.11).
  • The max_data_points that Hune sends for the panel (the body of 6.20), against the maxDataPoints of the Grafana request for the same panel and window. A difference gets the cause interval (19.31).
  • These rules of Grafana, which the project took from the source code only:
    • the field order of long-to-wide: the order of first appearance (9.22);
    • a regex mapping with $1 in its result text (9.17);
    • the order of the slices of a pie chart (9.19);
    • the unit tables: the text of each unit in use (9.15);
    • now/w starts on Monday at 00:00 (9.9).

The cells compare as texts, after the removal of white space at both ends. A difference gets one cause:

CauseMeaningAction
precisionAn integer above 2^53 that Grafana rounds.Expected (7.16). The operator signs the list.
formatA unit, decimal or mapping rule gives another text.Fix Hune, or the operator signs the cause.
data_movedA new run gives no difference.None.
otherAny other difference.Fix Hune before the cutover, or the operator signs the cause.

The converter tools/parity/to_grafana.py writes allowCustomValue for each query and custom variable: true for an open variable and false for a closed one. Grafana reads an absent member as true, so the converter never leaves it out. The capture then compares the same control on both sides. The fixture tests/fixtures/grafana/open_variables.json has one open and one closed variable of the kinds query and custom, and one text variable. It is the output of the converter for tests/fixtures/dashboards/open_variables.json.

The converter writes an interval variable as the type interval and a constant variable as the type constant. The fixture tests/fixtures/grafana/variable_kinds.json is its output for tests/fixtures/dashboards/variable_kinds.json. It leaves out the stored current and options of a constant, which only repeat the value. It drops the placement of the interval variable with a warning, as for any variable (4.7a).

15.14c Visual check

The capture tool takes a full-page screenshot of each dashboard in Grafana and in Hune. It uses range R2, set S1 and a window of 1920 by 1080 pixels. Hune gives one screenshot in the light theme and one in the dark theme. The operator reviews them side by side and writes OK or a defect for each panel. A defect needs a fix and a new review, or a signature of the operator.

The parity harness has three fixture dashboards for the repeat: tests/fixtures/parity/repeat-h.json, repeat-v.json and repeat-section.json. The first has a panel with h and max_per_row 3. The second has a panel with v and panels around it. The third has a section with a repeat. The converter tools/parity/to_grafana.py writes them with repeat (4.13a, 15.6).

The capture finds a panel of Grafana by data-viz-panel-key (panel-<id> and panel-<id>-clone-<n - 1>). It finds a panel of Hune by data-testid (panel-<id> and panel-<id>-r<n>). It compares the box of each copy, the title and the number of queries. The order of the copies is the order of the options in both, so the fixtures select all the values. A difference that 19.20b lists is not a fault.

The tool tools/parity/visual.mjs makes these comparisons. It takes one Grafana dashboard file and the uid of the same dashboard in Hune. It saves the file in the folder hune-parity of Grafana under the uid hpar-<uid>. It deletes the dashboard at the end. It deletes the folder too when it made the folder and the folder holds no other dashboard. Another run can use the folder at the same time, and the removal of a folder removes its dashboards.

Grafana 12.4.2 ignores milliseconds in from and to on a dashboard URL. So the tool writes the time of the Grafana URL as an ISO time in UTC.

The tool sets the window of Hune so that the widest panel without repeat has the same width in both products. The canvases then have the same size.

The tool pairs the copies by the id and the number of the copy. The copy k of Grafana (panel-<id>-clone-<k>) is the copy k + 1 of Hune (panel-<id>-r<k + 1>). In Grafana, a panel of a repeated section has the same key in each copy. The n-th element with this key is the copy n. The number of queries of a copy is the number of queries in its requests in Grafana. In Hune it is the number of requests that carry the value of the copy.

The option --replay DIR compares the captures that an earlier run saved in DIR/capture.json. It opens no browser and writes nothing to Grafana.

The option --grafana-datasource-map FROM=TO saves the dashboard with another data source uid. Use it for a source that the disposable Grafana does not have. The option --grafana-quote-placeholders writes each ${name} of a SQL text as ${name:singlequote} in Grafana. Hune quotes a value in a code context, and Grafana gives the raw text.

The hook tools/parity/canvas-hook.js records the calls of each canvas since its last clear. These are fill, fillRect, stroke, fillText, the rectangles of the paths and the clip rectangle.

The rules of tools/parity/visual-rules.mjs give each difference a class. A difference that a section of 19.20 lists has the class documented and the id of the section. Any other difference is a fault.

The file tests/fixtures/parity/visual-open-faults.json lists the faults that are open, each with its cause. A case fails on a fault that this file does not list. The cases B101p, B137 and B148 run the tool when the Grafana of 127.0.0.1:3010 answers.

The tolerance of a mark on a canvas is 1.5 px. This is the 1 px of 15.14c and 0.5 px for the snap of a cell of Hune to a device pixel. A plot that moves or scales by more than 2 px is a fact of its own, and the check plot reports it. The marks of Hune map into the plot of Grafana in x when the plots differ. They map in y when the heights differ by more than 4 px.

The library uPlot of Grafana clips a series to the plot and half its line width. Thus the plot of Grafana is its clip less half the widest line in the clip.

The bars of a histogram pair by their x. With a bucketOffset that is not a multiple of bucketSize, they pair by their x value, from the labels of each x axis (19.20g). The height of a bar compares as a share of the tallest bar. A time series whose y axes are all hidden has the check plot:top (E27, 9.24). It compares the top of the plot of the lines, within 2 px.

The parity harness has three fixture dashboards for the text panel. The first has each construct of 9.19b with no variable. The second has placeholders with plain values. The third has links, a table and a code block.

The converter tools/parity/to_grafana.py writes a text panel as the panel of Grafana. It writes options.mode = markdown, the joined content and the default options.code, and no targets.

The capture finds the root of Grafana by data-testid="TextPanel-converted-content". It finds the root of Hune by data-testid="text-<id>". It compares the box, the title, the text of the body and the sequence of element names. A difference that 19.20c lists is not a fault. The values of the variables in the fixtures hold no Markdown character, because Grafana replaces them before it parses.

The parity harness has the fixture dashboards of the two types. They cover the value mappings (value, range, regex and special). They cover a numeric field in the colour modes thresholds, fixed and continuous_gr_yl_rd, a text field, a bool field and several rows.

They also cover the null values and the gaps, and each option of 4.33b. They cover both legend places. The fixtures are 8 dashboards that the project measured on Grafana, saved as tests/fixtures/grafana/timelines_*.json.

The converter tools/parity/to_grafana.py writes the two types as the panels state-timeline and status-history, with the options and the custom members of 15.6. The capture records the canvas calls of both pages with one hook script (fill, stroke, fillText and the rectangles of the paths). It compares the boxes with a tolerance of 1 px, the colours, the texts and the tooltips. A difference that 19.20d lists is not a fault.

The parity harness has 4 fixture dashboards for the annotations. They are the files tests/fixtures/grafana/annotations_*.json: colours, filter, texts and many. Each one is the output of the converter for the document tests/fixtures/parity/annotations-*.json. The first has points and regions in 3 colours on 2 time series. It has a region that starts before the range and a region that ends after it.

The second has the filter panels and an annotation with enabled equal to false. The third has texts with a tag, a long text and a text with a line end. The fourth has 1,000 points.

The converter tools/parity/to_grafana.py writes the member annotations. Each annotation becomes an item of annotations.list. The item has datasource, enable, hide equal to false, iconColor (the text of the colour) and name.

It has target (made by target_out, with the format table and the ref id Anno). It has filter (exclude equal to false and ids) or no filter. The converter writes the built-in item too, with hide equal to true. The importer reads the output back to the same members, and it lists two items for each annotation: hide equal to false and the warning of 15.6.

The capture records the canvas calls of Grafana (the lines with setLineDash, the fills and the DOM markers annotation-marker) and the data data-annotations of Hune. It compares the x of each mark (1 px), the width of each region (1 px) and the colour of each line. It compares the number of marks. It also counts the requests: 1 annotation request for each annotation in both products. The differences of 19.20e are not faults.

Several queries (4.15a). The capture compares the data of a panel for each ref. The key of a record is the panel and the ref, as it was the panel and the refId before. Grafana sends one request for each panel and each data source, and Hune sends one for each query, so the count of requests differs. The report lists this difference once (19.20f). The capture pairs the request of Hune with the query of Grafana that has the same ref.

The visual capture of 15.14c reads the display names of the combined frame (A a, B b). It reads the legend items, the headers of a table and the select of the frames.

The converter tools/parity/to_grafana.py writes one target for each query, with refId, hide and the datasource of the source. It writes the data source -- Mixed -- for a panel whose queries have more than one source. It writes the matcher byFrameRefID for the matcher query. The 6 fixtures tests/fixtures/grafana/multi_query_*.json are the output of the converter for the documents tests/fixtures/parity/multi-query-*.json. They hold these panels:

  • a time series with 3 queries, and a table with 2 queries;
  • a stat with a hidden query, and a panel with Mixed sources;
  • a panel with an error in one query, and a panel with all queries hidden.

The parity harness has the fixture dashboards of the heatmap and of the histogram. The heatmap fixtures cover the shape that is already bucketed. They cover names that are numbers and names that are not, null cells, zero cells, one time row and irregular times. They also cover the shape of raw values, with each rule of the time size and of the value size and the three scales.

The fixtures also cover each colour option, each cell option, each place of the axis, the three modes of the card and the legend. The histogram fixtures cover each bucket option, combine, the three modes of stacking and the options of the line and the fill. They cover these cases of the data. They are negative values, two clusters with a gap, equal values, one value, null values and a very small bucket size.

The fixtures are 8 dashboards that the project measured on Grafana. They are in tests/fixtures/grafana/ as heatmap_*.json and histogram_*.json: the files heatmap_a, heatmap_b, heatmap_c, heatmap_d, heatmap_e, histogram_f, heatmap_g and histogram_h. Each panel has its own row (4.14), and the range is two epoch times in milliseconds (4.4).

The fixture tests/fixtures/grafana/parity_axes.json holds three time series with all their y axes hidden and one time series with an axis. It also holds the histograms of the measure of the y ticks, with the heights 5 and 16. The tool runs it by hand.

The converter tools/parity/to_grafana.py writes the two types as the panels heatmap and histogram, with the options and the custom members of 15.6. The capture records the canvas calls of both pages with one hook script (fill and the rectangles of the paths, fillText). It compares the boxes of the cells and of the bars with a tolerance of 1 px, the colours, the texts and the cards. A colour of the 9 exact schemes has a tolerance of 2 in each channel. A colour of viridis has a tolerance of 8. A difference that 19.20g lists is not a fault.

15.15 Coexistence, cutover and rollback

The cutover happens after the exit criteria of M4 (section 18). Hune then runs in volume storage and in mode open, behind the login of the reverse proxy that guards /grafana/ today. T is the day of the proxy switch. All dates below are durations from T. This runbook uses nginx and Docker Compose as examples. Adapt the commands to your set-up.

Preconditions:

  • The steps V1 to V5 of 15.14 passed. The export was at most 7 days old at T minus 14 days.
  • Each server action of the plan has a written recap before it runs, and the person who is responsible for the instance authorised it.
  • Nobody builds the Grafana image again from the start of V3 until T plus 30 days. When the image has no pinned version, a new build can change the Grafana and plugin versions that V3 measured.
  • A test environment ran the same plan first, with its T at least 7 days before the T of the production environment.
WhenStep
T − 14 dAdd the service hune to the compose file of the host, with the volume as a read-only store: no primary = true, no history_keep, and a title. Add the nginx location /dashboards/ (below). Run the first import with --write. The importer uses a second file, import.hcl, equal to the file of serve except that the volume has primary = true and history_keep, and has no title. A read-only serve takes no lock (5.19), so the importer takes it (5.16).
T − 14 d to T − 2 dParallel run. Grafana stays the editor and the source of truth. After each Grafana change, and at least one time each day, export and import again with --write --replace. Users compare the two tools. Hune fixes come as new releases. The operator reads the nginx log for /grafana/public-dashboards/ and /grafana/goto/ (19.2).
T − 2 dEdit freeze. Give the role Viewer to each Grafana user. When an environment variable of the Grafana service sets the role, create the container again without a build (docker compose up -d --no-deps grafana). Grafana then refuses saves. Tell the users about the freeze.
TFinal export and final import with --write --replace. Run V3 again for each dashboard that changed after the last V3. Give the volume of Hune primary = true and history_keep, remove its title, and start it again. Set GF_SERVER_ROOT_URL=/grafana-old/ on Grafana, create it again without a build, and route /grafana-old/ to it. Replace the nginx location /grafana/ with the redirects of 15.17.
T to T + 14 dGrafana stays reachable read-only at /grafana-old/, behind the same login, for comparison.
T + 14 dStop the Grafana container (docker compose stop grafana). Do not touch its data volume.
T + 30 dEnd of the rollback window. Remove the Grafana service and archive grafana.db (19.10).
T + 6 monthsRemove the redirect locations of 15.17.

The nginx location of Hune copies the access rule of the current /grafana/ location. With server.base_path set to /dashboards/ (section 13), nginx passes the path without a change:

nginx
location /dashboards/ {
    # Copy the access lines of the current location /grafana/ here, unchanged.
    auth_request /auth/verify;
    proxy_intercept_errors off;

    proxy_pass http://hune:8080;
    proxy_set_header Host $host;
    proxy_set_header X-Real-IP $remote_addr;
    proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-For $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for;
    proxy_read_timeout 120s;
}

From T to T + 14 d, the old Grafana answers under /grafana-old/ with the same access rule:

nginx
location /grafana-old/ {
    # Copy the access lines of the current location /grafana/ here, unchanged.
    auth_request /auth/verify;
    proxy_intercept_errors off;

    proxy_pass http://grafana:3000/grafana-old/;
    proxy_set_header Host $host;
    proxy_set_header X-Real-IP $remote_addr;
    proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-For $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for;
}

Rollback is possible from T to T + 30 d. These are its triggers:

  • A defect that stops the daily work, with no fix within 1 business day.
  • A panel that shows wrong data, with no fix within 1 business day.
  • A decision of the operator.

Rollback procedure:

  1. Remove primary = true and history_keep from the store of Hune, give it a title, and start it again. This stops new edits.
  2. Run hune export -c <file> --to <dir> (5.38) to a private directory. It keeps the edits that people made in Hune after T.
  3. Give the Grafana users their roles of before the freeze again. Remove GF_SERVER_ROOT_URL from the Grafana service. Start it again without a build.
  4. Restore the old nginx location /grafana/. Remove the redirect locations. Run nginx -t, then reload nginx.
  5. Tell the users about the rollback. People apply their Hune edits in Grafana by hand. Hune never writes to Grafana.

15.16 Changes after the cutover

After T, Hune is the source of truth. The importer is not used again for these dashboards. A dashboard that someone exports from another Grafana later uses the same command, with a new acceptance file.

15.17 The nginx redirect map

The redirects keep the old links usable for 6 months: bookmarks, chat links and the short links. --nginx-map <file> writes the two maps below. The operator includes the file in the http context of nginx.

nginx
# Written by hune import-grafana. Include in the http context.
map $hune_grafana_uid $hune_new_uid {
    default "";
    "abc123" "abc123";
    "Nodes_Prod" "nodes-prod";
}
map $hune_grafana_short $hune_short_target {
    default "";
    "xYz12AB" "/dashboards/d/abc123/nodes?from=now-7d&to=now&var-node=%24__all";
}

The operator writes these locations in the server context once. They replace the location /grafana/ at T:

nginx
location ~ ^/grafana/d/(?<hune_grafana_uid>[A-Za-z0-9_-]+)(/[^?]*)?$ {
    if ($hune_new_uid = "") { return 302 /dashboards/; }
    return 301 /dashboards/d/$hune_new_uid$is_args$args;
}
location ~ ^/grafana/goto/(?<hune_grafana_short>[A-Za-z0-9_-]+)$ {
    if ($hune_short_target = "") { return 302 /dashboards/; }
    return 302 $hune_short_target;
}
location /grafana/ {
    return 302 /dashboards/;
}

The rules of the map:

  • A dashboard line maps each imported Grafana uid to its Hune uid (15.8). The redirect keeps the query string. Hune uses the Grafana query names from, to, var-<name>, refresh and viewPanel, and drops orgId (9.5).
  • A short-link line maps the short uid to its target. The importer reads path of short-urls.json. A path d/<grafana uid>[/<slug>][?<query>] becomes <new>d/<hune uid>[/<slug>][?<query>], with <new> from --link-prefix. Another path becomes <new> and an item of class changed.
  • The importer percent-encodes seven characters in a target: $, ", \, space, ;, { and }. Their codes are %24, %22, %5C, %20, %3B, %7B and %7D. Reason: nginx reads $ in a map value as a variable.
  • A Grafana uid or a short uid with a character outside [A-Za-z0-9_-] gets no line. It gets an item of class changed.

nginx runs return in its rewrite phase, before auth_request. So the redirects answer without the login check. They give no data: the target location /dashboards/ checks the login.

The importer also rewrites data links of the documents with --link-prefix. A link that starts with <old>d/<grafana uid> becomes <new>d/<hune uid> with the rest of the link unchanged. Without --link-prefix, such a link stays as it is and gets an item of class changed.

15.18 Confidentiality

Real dashboards hold the business SQL of the instance. These things never enter the repository of Hune, an issue, a pull request or a CI log:

  • the exports of 15.3 and the copies of grafana.db;
  • the reports of real runs, the acceptance files and the nginx maps of real runs;
  • the requests, answers, CSV files and screenshots of 15.14;
  • any SQL text of a real dashboard.

They stay on a trusted machine or a private runner, in a directory outside every git checkout, with mode 0700. The importer and the tools of tools/parity/ take every path from their command line, and the test parity_private from HUNE_PARITY_DIR. None of them has a default path. They refuse an output path inside a git checkout (15.2).

Synthetic fixtures in tests/fixtures/grafana/ cover each row of 15.6 (16.13). The policy check of 16.18 refuses a fixture that names a production database or a host of a real deployment.

16. Tests

16.1 Rules for every test

  • Every test runs in Docker. The CI runner needs only git, Docker Engine and the Docker Compose plugin.
  • Each image that a test uses has a tag and a digest in tests/images.lock. One line holds <name> <image>@sha256:<digest>. The test scripts take images from this file only.
  • No test connects to a server of a real deployment. No test holds a credential, an address or the data of a real deployment. The CI holds no credential of a real deployment. The verification of 15.14 is not a test. It is a manual step.
  • A test is deterministic, or it uses a seed and prints the seed when it fails.
  • A failed test prints the last 200 log lines of each container of its compose project.
  • Each test project has the compose project name hune-<job>-<run id>. It removes only its own containers, networks and volumes.
  • A case that expects an error checks the HTTP status and the error code of section 6 for the situation. The tables below name the situation.
  • The builder image hune-builder comes from build/Dockerfile.build (17.2). The jobs run cargo only inside it.

The annotations from PostgreSQL and MongoDB sources add these tests:

  • Browser: the case B228. The cases B01, B34, B50 to B52 and B91 follow the new page.

It tests the line diff for the marks of the gutter. The cases are an added, a modified and a deleted line.   • Browser: the cases B240, B270, B271 and B272.

The select and copy rules (9.8e) add these tests to the groups of the table:

It tests the order of the texts, the signature and the cap of 400 texts. It tests the wrapper of fillText and clearRect on a fake context. It tests the text of a copy.

The default range and the default refresh (10.9) add these tests to the groups of the table:

The ad hoc filters (F36) add these tests to the groups of the table:

16.4 Seeded random tests of the lexer and the substitution

The generator is a dev-dependency only. It uses a seeded pseudo-random generator, so a seed gives the same cases on each platform. The tests are in crates/hune-core/tests/random.rs, so the environment check of 16.18 does not apply to them.

The generator makes SQL templates from these parts:

  • keywords, plain identifiers, and identifiers in backquotes and in double quotes, with escapes;
  • numbers, and string literals with \', '' and \\;
  • line comments (-- and #) and nested block comments;
  • heredoc text ($tag$ ... $tag$);
  • the macros of section 7.

The generator puts variable references in code, in string literals, in comments, in identifiers and in heredoc text. The references are $v, ${v} and each format form of section 7.

The generator makes values from an alphabet that favours dangerous characters: ', ", ` , \, $, {, }, -, /, *, #, ;, line ends, tabulators, NUL, U+2028 and characters outside the Basic Multilingual Plane. A value has 0 to 1024 bytes. A multi-value list has 0 to 50 values.

PropertyStatement
R1A value never leaves its literal. The lexer reads the output again. At each place of a reference, the output holds one string literal (or a list of literals and commas for several values). The decoded literal equals the value, byte for byte.
R2The tokens outside the places of references are equal to the tokens of the template: same kinds and same text.
R3A value that the rules of section 7 refuse gives an error and no SQL: free text in an identifier, NUL, a raw value outside its closed list.
R4A reference in a comment stays as text.
R5The same inputs give the same bytes.
R6The output passes the statement check of section 7 when the template passes it, and fails when the template fails.
R7The lexer never panics. The concatenation of the token texts equals the input.
R8A format of the last group of 7.9 never puts free text outside its literal. In a string, the output holds one string literal at the place of the reference, and the decoded literal equals the text of the format with the value. In code and in a quoted name, the same reference gives an error, at save and at run.
R9$__conditionalAll: with "All", the output holds 1=1 and none of the first argument. Else property R1 holds for each free value of the first argument.

The properties R1 to R9 run for each dialect. The generator of the dialect postgres adds these pieces: E strings, dollar quotes, U& forms, a backslash before a quote mark, #, Unicode spaces and runs of operator bytes. For this dialect, R3 also puts a free value in an E string, in a prefixed string and in a dollar quote. R6 uses the statement check of 7.25.

The kind mongodb has its own generator and the property R10 in place of R1 to R9. The generator makes query trees from the stages and the operators of 7.31. It puts whole-leaf and embedded placeholders at all value places, in keys and in name places.

The values are hostile: $, $$ROOT, $secret, ., {"$out": "x"}, a JSON text, quote marks, NUL, U+2028 and long texts. The property R10 has three parts:

  • The expansion gives an error. Else it gives a tree T1 with the keys, the stage names and the operator names of T0 (the property PM of 7.35).
  • Each leaf that holds a free value does not start with $.
  • A placeholder in a key or in a name place always gives an error.

The same inputs give the same bytes. The parser of 7.28 never panics, and it gives the same tree for the same text.

Each seeded test runs two times for a custom variable: once closed and once open, with the same values. For a query variable it runs two times: with allow_custom_value true and false. The property P, PG or PM holds in each run, and the output of the two runs of a query variable is the same.

The rust job runs the seeds 1 to 64 with 5,000 cases each. The nightly job runs 1,000 seeds with 20,000 cases each, and the seeds come from the date. A failure prints the seed and the smallest failed case that the shrink step finds. The command below runs one seed again:

sh
docker run --rm -v "$PWD:/src" -w /src -e HUNE_TEST_SEED=4242 hune-builder \
  cargo test -p hune-core --locked random_

The ClickHouse case C03 (16.9) adds a check against the real ClickHouse lexer: ClickHouse returns each value of a sample, unchanged.

16.5 Configuration tests

CaseCheck
K01For each key of the table of section 13: the minimum and the maximum are accepted. A value below or above stops the start. The error names the key, the expected type or range, and the value.
K02A wrong type stops the start, with no coercion: "8080" for an integer, 1.0 for an integer, "true" for a boolean.
K03An unknown block, an unknown attribute, a duplicate attribute and a duplicate block label each stop the start.
K04<name>, <name>_env and <name>_file: exactly one form is accepted. Two forms stop the start. A variable that does not exist, an empty value, or a file that the program cannot read stop the start, and the error names the attribute and the variable or the path. A _file value loses its last line end only.
K05A secret in clear text in the file stops the start. print-config and the start log hide each secret.
K06Each contradiction of section 13 stops the start. The error names both keys and both values.
K07A key of the mode authzen with the mode open stops the start. A necessary key that is absent stops the start.
K08An expression, a function call, an interpolation "${x}" and a heredoc each stop the start.
K09A duration with a fraction, a sign, two units or no unit stops the start.
K10check-config on a file with 5 errors prints 5 errors and exits 1. On a good file it prints nothing and exits 0.
K11print-config prints the value and the source (file, env:VAR, file:/path) of each key and exits 0. It runs in a container with --network none, so it contacts no network.
K12The start log line of section 14 holds the same values as print-config.
K13The output of hune print-reference --format markdown equals the committed docs/configuration-reference.md (13.21).
K14These variables, set to bad values, change nothing: RUST_LOG, OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT, OTEL_SDK_DISABLED, OTEL_SERVICE_NAME, HTTP_PROXY, HTTPS_PROXY, ALL_PROXY, NO_PROXY, SSL_CERT_FILE, SSL_CERT_DIR. The test compares the output of print-config and the behaviour of a panel query. It is the test environment_has_no_effect of 13.24, and it runs in the compose project of 16.8 (job api). It has three parts: K14a in the mode open (from M2), K14b in the mode authzen with fake-pdp (from M6), K14c with telemetry.enabled = true (from M7, a build with the OTLP exporter).

K16The block postgres "<id>": each key at its minimum and its maximum; each form of 13.5 for user and password; a secret in clear text; the contradictions C14 to C20; the warning W7; the output of print-config for the example of 13.12a; the tables of 13.12a in the reference document.

An absent key has its default with the source default. A key in the file gives its value with the source file. The keys are in the key table, in the reference and in the order of print-config.
