B242A file of the read-only store that is not valid after the start (9.7, 9.8). The API gives read_only, store and stale, with no path. The card has the warning mark and the lock. The page has the warning notice, the lock and no switch "Edit". The mark goes when the file is valid again.
B243The header selector (9.6). The read-only store is a row with a lock. A click opens its two folders, and a click on a dashboard of the store opens it.
B244A write to the read-only store (6.8). The API gives 403 read_only_store for a create, a save and a delete, and the primary accepts a write. The page of the dashboard with edit=1 has no edit control and has the lock.
B260Select and copy a legend (9.8e). A drag over the text of a legend item selects the text and toggles no series. A plain click toggles the series, and a second click gives the first state again. A triple click selects the text. Ctrl+C on a focused item with no selection copies its text.
B261Select and copy a link (9.8e). A drag over the text of a link in a table cell selects the text and does not follow the link. A plain click follows the link.
B262Select and copy the text of a chart (9.8e). The line chart, the bar chart and the pie chart each have a layer of 1 to 400 texts that is hidden from assistive technology. A drag over the labels of the vertical axis selects them, and a copy gives one label on each line. A triple click selects a text of the pie chart. The tooltip shows over the plot, and the point of the plot is not under the layer.
B263Select and copy a header (9.8e). A drag over a sortable header selects its text and does not sort. A plain click sorts. A drag over two cells selects text.
B264Select and copy the lines of a logs panel (9.8e). A drag inside one cell uses the copy of the browser. A drag over a line, over two lines and from the middle of a cell copies one text line for each log line, with one space between the cells. A triple click selects the whole line and leaves the details closed, or open when they were open. A double click leaves the details as they were. A plain click toggles them. Ctrl+A with the focus on a line copies the six lines.
B265Select and copy the rows of other panels (9.8e). A triple click and a drag over two lines of a logs panel with wrap copy one text line for each line. A drag over table cells uses the copy of the browser, with a tab and a line end. The rows of the horizontal bar chart and of the bar gauge copy as the name and the value on one text line.
B266The cursor over text and the right button (9.8e). The cursor is the text cursor over a log line, a legend label, a table cell, a panel title and an axis label, and the pointer over a sortable header and a legend item. The right button on selected log lines, a legend label, axis labels and table cells keeps the selection and toggles no line, no series and no sort. No code of the page stops the menu of the browser.
B270The look of the JSON view (10.4). In the light and the dark theme, each kind of text and each depth of bracket has the colour of its token. The editor, the line numbers, the selection with and without focus, and the scroll bar of 10 px have the values of the palettes. The layers take no pointer.
B271The caret in the JSON view (10.4). A click puts the band and the strong number on its line. A caret that touches a bracket puts a box on it and on its pair, and the guide runs along the lines between them. A selection has no band and no box. A line with six spaces has a guide box of six columns and one row high. A syntax error has a wavy underline under its token, and it goes when the text is valid.
B272The layout of the page (9.6). At 1440x900, 1280x720, 768x900 and 390x800, in the light and the dark theme, on the list, on a dashboard, in the view of a panel, in the edit mode, in the panel editor and in the JSON view: the header has the width of the window and is at the top, #app-main starts under it and is the one scroll container (positioned, overflow: auto), the document has no vertical scrollbar and is not taller than the window, and nothing scrolls on the x axis. A low list scrolls in the container and the header keeps its place. The row of the variables stays under the header after a scroll. A drag of a panel near the bottom edge scrolls the container and not the document.
B280The preview of a time series (9.11). The queries are held. The preview has three lines, an area, five labels of the y axis and six of the x axis, and no dots. The legend follows the options: a list at the bottom, a list at the right, a table at the bottom, a table at the right, and off. A hidden y axis has no labels. After the load, the plot of the preview and the plot of the chart differ by 4 px at most in left, top, width and height. For a legend at the right, the width differs by 30 px at most.
B281The preview of a bar chart and of a pie chart (9.11). A vertical bar chart with a legend at the bottom, a horizontal bar chart with its values, and a pie with the legend at the bottom, at the right, as a table at the right and off. After the load, the plot of the preview and the plot of the chart differ by 4 px at most (30 px in width for a legend at the right).
B282The preview of each panel type (9.11). A stat with a line, three gauges, four bar gauges in columns, a table, a heatmap with its colour scale, a histogram, a state timeline with no legend, and a stacked bar series with no line. The light has the animation panel-skel, and the reduced motion stops it. A shape has a visible colour in both themes. A reload of a loaded panel shows no preview.
B300The list page with dashboards with no folder (9.7). The root group of the primary is first and has no title. The folder below it keeps its title. The root group of the read-only store is the first group under its title, with a lock and no menu. GET api/folders has the member root and no root item.
B301The dialog New dashboard (10.7). Its select has "Root (no folder)" first, and the folders after it. A dashboard that the dialog makes has the folder null in the API, and it shows in the root group.
B302The dialog Import (10.12). Its select has "Root (no folder)" first. A file that the dialog imports there has the folder null.
B303The dialog Move (10.7). A dashboard in a folder can move to the root. A dashboard at the root has no root entry and moves to a folder. The API shows both moves.
B304The header selector (9.6). The dashboards with no folder are lines on their own, first, then the folder row and the store row. The root dashboard of the read-only store comes before its folders.
B305A server with no primary store (11.25). The member root is read-only and belongs to no store. The root dashboards show in the block of the store with a lock and no menu, and no New button shows.

A build made with --no-default-features has no MongoDB driver and refuses the mongodb block at start (8b.2). The runner then reports B47 and B48 as skip, with the reason "no MongoDB driver in this build". It detects this case from the exit status and the message of hune check-config. A build with the driver (the default build) runs both cases against a disposable MongoDB 8.0 with openssl and docker.

On a failure, the job keeps the screenshots, the traces and the console log of the failed case as CI artefacts. The tests use no pixel baseline.

16.10a Front-end units

The pure functions of the browser have their own layer: the functions of 9.9, of 9.12 to 9.23 and of 10.15. The job frontend runs node --test tests/frontend/ in the browser test image (16.10). It needs no browser and no binary.

  • Each function has golden vectors in tests/frontend/vectors/<function>.json: an input, the options and the expected output. Section 9 gives the id of each vector set.
  • The functions include these:
    • the date math and the time zones (9.9), and the frame to fields (9.12);
    • the display names (9.13), the field configuration and the matchers (9.14);
    • the units and decimals (9.15), the colours and thresholds (9.16), and the value mappings (9.17);
    • the data-link tokens (9.18), the transformations and the long-to-wide (9.21, 9.22), and the panel reuse (9.23);
    • the line diff (10.15).
  • The vector sets FE-06 and FE-14 hold the plural time words of 9.15 and the series order of 9.22. The tests read the vector files as they are. They keep no list of deviations.
  • These cases are in the sets that exist:
    • FE-06: the 21 new units. Each one has a small, a large, a negative and a zero value, and a case with decimals.
    • FE-06, the 30 units of 4.20: the file FE-06-units-h.json and the test fe06_units_h.test.mjs. The file holds the texts of Grafana 12.4.2 for the 30 new units of 4.20. It has 30 values and the decimals absent, 0 and 2, from the measure of 2026-11-22.
    • The same file holds the display of a number, a text and a bool value for each unit. The member grafana marks the 20 cases where the page differs on purpose (19.20n). The test also compares the 59 ids of 4.20 in four places: docs/02, hune-core, the check of the editor and its unit list.
    • The test barchart_p03.test.mjs: the bar chart and the engine edit E34 on a fake of the canvas. The fill has the alpha fill_opacity / 100, and the line has the plain colour at line_width, as in the probe of Grafana 12.4.2. The values 0 give no call, and each side of the box of a bar moves in by half the line width. axis_grid = false removes the grid lines in both orientations. The rule of auto is width < height, and a resize across the rule draws the chart again. 9 of its 10 tests fail before the change.
    • FE-04: the variables in titles. FE-05: the matcher names and the property hide_from.
    • FE-10: the new calculations. This includes the example of delta in 9.19 and a field with null, NaN or no row.
    • FE-11: the footer lines.
    • The test table_inspect.test.mjs: the text of the drawer "Inspect value" of 9.20. It holds the raw text, the indent of a JSON text, the depth limit, and the limit of 1,048,576 code units with its note. It also holds hostile values with markup in a fake document.
    • FE-01: the zone names (isKnownZone, a clock change, the label of 9.9).
    • FE-01, url_state.test.mjs and runtime.test.mjs (9.5): the three time forms of Grafana in the URL and the bounds of 4.4. They also hold the forms that Grafana reads as 1970 and the dates that do not exist. They hold two timezone names and a zone of the URL in the runtime.
    • The same tests hold hostile texts of 1,000,000 characters and the cut of the error text. The time is the minimum of 7 runs after 2 warm-ups.
    • The tests editor_props.test.mjs and editor_forms.test.mjs. The property catalog (4.18) agrees with tests/fixtures/dashboards/f2_members.json. Each form writes only members of section 4.
  • These eight vector sets exist:
    • FE-18: the CSV file of 9.11.
    • FE-19: the sort of the legend table and of the tooltip rows (9.19).
    • FE-20: the lines and the bands of the thresholds (9.19).
    • FE-21: formatTime of 9.19 in each zone.
    • FE-22: the range of a panel with a time override and its note (9.8b). It holds the cases of time_from and time_shift. It holds an absolute range of the dashboard, a clock change, a range below 0 and now/d at the first millisecond.
    • FE-23: the colour modes (9.16). It holds the 36 hex values of the series palette. It holds the stops and the interpolation of each continuous mode in both themes, and the values null and NaN.
    • FE-24: the axis plan (9.19). It holds the side for auto, left, right and hidden, three units, the range of a scale with hard and soft ends, and the title.
    • FE-25: the URLs and the lists of the dashboard links (9.8a). It holds the tokens, include_vars, keep_time, a fragment, a refused URL, the filter by tags, the current dashboard and the order.
    • FE-26: the states of the page (10.2a). The file tests/frontend/vectors/FE-26-view-state.json has three sets of vectors. The first set holds the URLs of rule 1: each level, two names, three names, an unknown id, an unknown value, panel-<id> and no right. The second set holds the rows of the second table of 10.2a. Each vector has the next state, the history action, the origin and the dialog. The third set holds the leave target for each origin.
    • FE-27: the repeat (4.13a, 9.8c). The file tests/frontend/vectors/FE-27-repeat.json has six sets.
      • The first set holds the values. It has the order of the options, "All", a subset, a custom variable and a query variable. It has a value that is not an option, and a selection that is the same set in two orders.
      • The second set holds the states and the limits. It has 0, 1, 24 and 25 values, and a section with 12 and with 13 values. It has the walk of the budget of the dashboard for three layouts of repeats.
      • The third set holds the layout. It has 40 vectors of direction h (k from 1 to 25, max_per_row from 1 to 24 and absent) and of direction v. It has the moves of the other panels, with the vectors that match the measures of Grafana 12.4.2.
      • The fourth set holds the keys and the URL forms: <id>, <id>-r<n>, panel-<id>-r<n> and <x>$panel-<id>. It also holds the values that the page refuses, such as an id 0 or an id that starts with a zero.
      • The fourth set also holds the copy that the form <x>$panel-<id> of Grafana names (10.2a, rule 1). It has text values, number values, the first copy and a section copy. It has texts that name no copy. These are an index, the option text, another case and a value that is not selected. It has a value with markup and a value with the characters of a pattern. A test checks that the form waits while the values load (rule 16).
      • The fifth set holds the request. It has the member variables of each copy, with the repeat variable fixed.
      • The sixth set holds the queries of a trigger, with a fake API. k copies give k requests. 6 requests at most are open at the same time. The states repeat-empty and repeat-limit give no request. A repeat of an unknown variable, or of a variable that cannot repeat, gives repeat-empty and no request. A limit of the session that is present and not valid gives repeat-limit and no copy. It also covers the abort at a change, and the count after a scroll.
    • The test tests/frontend/repeat.test.mjs runs FE-27. It checks that repeat.js has no DOM access and that its default constants equal the table of 4.35. The limits in force come from the member repeat_limits of the session (6.10), and the module reads them as an argument. It checks that the functions run in 50 ms or less for 200 panels and 120 copies. It checks that the module is in the list of the pure layer (static_rules.test.mjs).
    • The tests editor_forms.test.mjs and view_state.test.mjs get the cases of the group "Repeat options" and of rule 16 of 10.2a.
    • FE-28: the grammar of the Markdown (9.19b). The file tests/frontend/vectors/FE-28-markdown.json has 7 sets.
      • The sets are the blocks (140 vectors), the inline constructs (140 vectors) and the links with the autolinks (60 vectors, with the check of checkUrl).
      • The other sets are the tables, the tight and loose items, the limits and the escapes. Each limit has a vector at the maximum and a vector one above.
      • Each vector has the lines and the expected tree. The test helper serialize writes a tree as text, with the escape of &, < and ". It is not part of the page.
    • FE-29: the hostile vectors. The file tests/frontend/vectors/FE-29-markdown-hostile.json has 80 vectors.
      • They are the 40 payloads of B103 and a list of mutation attacks.
      • They are also the destinations with a space, a control character, a bidi character, a zero-width character, %0a or a backslash. Other vectors have a link with " or <, and a text with U+0000.
      • For each vector the test builds the DOM with a fake document and checks the elements, the attributes, the destinations and the text.
    • FE-30: the variables in a text (9.19b). The file tests/frontend/vectors/FE-30-text-vars.json has the forms of the table: a single value, several values, "All" with and without all_value, and a text variable.
      • It has the names that stay as written and the escape \$. It has the places that change: a leaf, a code span, a code block and a cell. It has the place that stays as written: a destination.
      • It has the hostile values of B104. For each one, the tree before and after the replacement has the same shape.
    • FE-31: the states and the segments (9.19c, sections 2 to 4). The file tests/frontend/vectors/FE-31-timeline-states.json has 5 sets.
      • The sets are the states of a value, the merge, span_nulls, the gaps and the cells. The states cover the mappings, the colour modes, the step texts, the empty text, bool, null and NaN.
      • It has 20 vectors that come from the measure of Grafana. Each one has the frame, the options, the x range, and the expected segments (x and width in pixels, 1 px tolerance), colours and texts.
      • The other vectors give the cases where Hune differs on purpose: the merge by state, the mapping before the step, and the bool colour.
    • FE-32: the geometry (9.19c, sections 6 and 7). The file FE-32-timeline-geometry.json has the rows for 1 to 40 rows and for each row_height. It has the left gutter for 20 names and the cell width for each col_width.
      • It also has the number of characters for 60 widths, and the ink for 40 fills in both themes. The vectors for the characters have the measured pairs of widths and counts.
    • FE-33: the caps and the hostile texts (9.19c, section 5, and T58). The file FE-33-timeline-caps.json has the counts at 9,999, 10,000 and 10,001. It has the rule of half the width at 491 and 492 rows for a width of 982, and the message texts.
      • It has 30 hostile state texts. The test builds the legend and the card with a fake document. It checks that each text is a text node and that no element comes from data.
    • FE-34: the duration text and the legend (9.19c, sections 9 and 10). The file FE-34-timeline-text.json has 60 durations from 0 ms to 3 years and the legend for each colour mode.
    • The tests are tests/frontend/timeline_model.test.mjs (FE-31, FE-32, FE-34) and timeline_dom.test.mjs (FE-33). A test also runs the model for 10,000 and 100,000 samples and checks the time (50 ms and 200 ms on the reference machine).
    • The test static_rules.test.mjs lists timeline_model in the pure layer. It checks that the constants of timeline_model.js equal 9.19c.

  • The tree of the query text (F42, 10.3c to 10.3g). The vector sets FE-55 to FE-67 are files of tests/fixtures/ast/. Each case has an input text, a dialect and an expected outline. The outline has the node types with their offsets, the clause names, the foldable marks and the pairs. The outline is the contract. The tests do not compare the whole object.
    • FE-55: ClickHouse WITH and CTE. One CTE, three CTEs, a scalar WITH 1 AS x and a CTE that holds a CTE. A ) that is not there, and a name that is not there.

Typed objects ($date, $oid) and the placeholders "$host" and "$__timeFrom". A comma at the end of a list, a duplicate member, a ] that is not there, and a value after the first one. Each gives error nodes and no exception.
    • FE-62: the caps. The sizes 262,144 and 262,145 bytes, with ASCII and with 2-byte, 3-byte and 4-byte characters. The depths 128 and 129. The node counts 100,000 and 100,001. Each case has the reason and the at offset.
    • FE-63: the SQL format for ClickHouse. Goldens for rules 1 to 13 of 10.3d, each with input and output. Each output formats to itself.
    • FE-64: the SQL format for PostgreSQL. Goldens for $$, ::, JSON operators, WITH RECURSIVE, RETURNING and a # operator.
    • FE-65: the JSON format. Goldens for 2 spaces, an object of 79, 80 and 81 columns, an array that fills, nested pipelines, and the refusals.
    • FE-66: the structure functions. folds, bracketPartner and blockAt for each caret offset of 30 texts. An offset outside the text (-1 and the length plus 1) gives null.
    • FE-67: odd texts. An empty text, a text of blanks, a BOM, U+0000, a lone surrogate, a pair of surrogates and bidi controls. A TAB, a line of 100,000 characters, and 10,000 open brackets. The result is a tree or a cap, never an exception.
    • The tests are tests/frontend/ast_sql.test.mjs (FE-55 to FE-59), ast_pg.test.mjs (FE-60), ast_json.test.mjs (FE-61), ast_format.test.mjs (FE-63 to FE-65), ast_structure.test.mjs (FE-66) and ast_props.test.mjs (FE-62, FE-67 and the properties below).
    • The test ast_props.test.mjs runs seeded random texts, with the seeds 1 to 8 and 2,000 texts for each seed and dialect. The alphabet holds the keywords, the brackets, the quotes, the comment starts, the macros, the variables, the dollar quotes and non-ASCII characters. The text is built from 1 to 80 pieces.
      • A1: no function of the tree throws, and the input is not changed.
      • A2: the tokens cover the text with no gap and no overlap, and each position is inside the text.
      • A3: each child is inside its parent, and the children of a node are in order and do not overlap.
      • A4: each code bracket belongs to exactly one node with open or close, or to an error node. No bracket of a string or a comment does.
      • A5: a macro and a variable are one node or one token, and their bytes equal the text.
      • A6: the time is linear. The parse of a text takes at most 2.5 times as long as the text of half the size. The sizes are 32,768, 65,536, 131,072 and 262,144 bytes. The last text takes 100 ms or less (the median of five runs).
      • A7: a text over a cap gives capped with the right reason, and no other result.
      • A8: two runs on the same text give equal trees.
      • A9: the format of a result that is not refused keeps the sequence of the non-blank tokens, with the same kinds and texts.
      • A10: the format is idempotent.
      • A11: a comment, a string, a name, a macro and a variable keep their bytes after a format. A line comment is always followed by a line end or by the end of the text.
      • A12: a refused format returns the input, and gives a reason and a place.
      • A13: the layout does not depend on the number of blanks. A text and the same text with each blank run changed to another blank run give the same result. The two runs have a line end in the same places.
      • A14: a text with random comments and random blanks (3,000 texts) is formatted or refused, never changed in its tokens.
    • The test static_rules.test.mjs lists editor/ast/ in the pure layer. It fails on a DOM name in these modules (document, window, Node, Range). It fails on a recursion on the depth of the input. It fails on an import of these modules from a module of the page. It checks that the constants of nodes.js equal the table of 10.3c.
    • The test ast_props.test.mjs also runs the parse and the format of 262,144 bytes, and checks the times of 9.30 with the limits of rule A6.
    • The tool tests/frontend/tools/ast_fuzz.mjs and its copy tools/ast_fuzz.mjs run the properties A1 to A14 with a seed and a count from their arguments. They print the seed and the text of the first failure, as markdown_fuzz.mjs does. A person runs the tool by hand. The job nightly does not run it yet.

    • FE-73: merge and join_by_field on lists of frames. 60 vectors from the measure (m01 to m13 and j01 to j23). They cover the keys, the conflicts, the null keys and the order of the keys. They also cover the product of inner, the frames with no key, and the refs and prefixes of 9.22a.
    • FE-74: reduce and partition_by_values. 30 vectors (the titles, the kinds of the reducer fields, a text field as a row, the labels, the order of first appearance, keep_fields).
    • FE-75: the caps and the time. MAX_TRANSFORM_ROWS, MAX_TRANSFORM_FIELDS, MAX_TRANSFORM_FRAMES, MAX_TRANSFORM_CELLS and MAX_MERGE_STEPS each at the bound and one above. A merge counts one step for each field of a compare. A join that would pass a cap fails before it builds a row.
    • FE-75 also checks the cap of the cells, which is the member transform_max_cells of the session. A configured value applies. An absent or bad value fails closed. No file of web/js holds a copy of 10,000,000.
    • The timing test of FE-75: join_by_field and merge for 200,000 rows in all, sort_by for 200,000 rows, convert_field_type and calculate_field for 200,000 rows, each in 300 ms or less.
    • A measure file tests/frontend/vectors/measure-f28.json holds 237 cases measured on Grafana (the input spec, the Grafana output). The test runs the prototype on each case and checks that the 60 differences are the ones that 19.20j lists.
  • The vectors of drag and resize are in tests/frontend/editor_drag.test.mjs and tests/frontend/editor_drag_pixels.test.mjs. They hold the layouts of Grafana for a path of cells, the clamp ranges and the pixel rules.
  • Three tests of tests/frontend/grid_frame.test.mjs check the menu item Inspect: inspectAllowed and inspectModel of web/js/panel_frame.js (9.11).
  • The test tests/frontend/engine_removals.test.mjs checks the removals E17 to E21 and E35 to E42 of the copied engine (9.24). Its lists name the helpers of app_util.js (E35), the bucket readout (E36), the colour kernel (E37) and the members of the state shim (E38). They also name the chart card (E39) and the alignment groups (E40). For E41, they name the legend filter, the totals legend and the mode button, and for E42 ten options that Hune never sets. It fails when a removed function comes back as a call in its engine file, or a removed member as a call in web/js. It also fails on a removed engine option in a panel, and on an import of a removed name or of the shim app_ui_copy.js.
  • The regular expressions of the fixtures run in two engines: the check of hune-core (Rust) and the RegExp of the page (Node). Each pattern gives the same accept or refuse result and the same matches in both (19.30).
  • The vectors of a Grafana rule come from Grafana itself, through the capture of 16.13, where Grafana can give them.

16.11 PostgreSQL failover test

The test proves that a failover of the primary loses no acknowledged save, makes no duplicate, and stops no read. It runs in the failover job (16.17). The CloudNativePG drill of 16.19 completes it before 1.0.

The compose project tests/failover/compose.yaml holds these services:

ServiceRole
etcdThe configuration store of Patroni, one node.
patroni-1, patroni-2, patroni-3PostgreSQL 18 with Patroni, built from tests/failover/Dockerfile.patroni. Settings: ttl: 10, loop_wait: 2, retry_timeout: 4, synchronous_mode: true, use_pg_rewind: true.
haproxyThe one address of the primary, as the -rw service of CloudNativePG. It checks GET /primary on port 8008 of each node every 1 s and closes the sessions of a node that goes down.
hune-a, hune-bTwo instances with storage.postgres.host = "haproxy", poll_interval = "1s", statement_timeout = "2s".
driverThe load and the journal: the integration test failover of the crate hune.
checkerThe checks of the end, with psql.

The script tests/failover/run.sh <mode> runs the steps. The mode kill sends SIGKILL to the primary. The mode partition removes the primary from the network without a TCP reset, as a cut node does. The job runs both modes.

  1. Start etcd, the three Patroni nodes and haproxy. Wait until Patroni reports one leader and two replicas.
  2. Through haproxy, as the superuser, create the two roles of 5.25. Run hune migrate-store -c migrate.hcl once with the owner role, then give the grants of 5.25 to the application role. Start hune-a and hune-b with the application role. Wait until /readyz gives 200 on both.
  3. Create 20 synthetic dashboards through hune-a.
  4. Start the driver. Every 200 ms it saves a random dashboard, on hune-a and hune-b in turn, each save with a new save_id. On an answer 503 it sends the same request again every 1 s, for at most 120 s. The request keeps its save_id and its If-Match. Every 100 ms it reads the list and one dashboard on both instances. It writes each request and answer to its journal.
  5. After 30 s of load, find the leader with the Patroni REST API and apply the mode to it.
  6. Wait until Patroni reports a new leader (at most 60 s). Record the time.
  7. Keep the load for 60 s more. Then start the old leader again, or connect it again. Wait until it streams as a replica (at most 120 s).
  8. Stop the driver. Wait 2 s, then run the checker.
Pass criterionCheck
F1No acknowledged save is lost. For each save with answer 200 in the journal, the history of its uid holds exactly one version with its save_id. The last etag of each uid equals the etag of its last acknowledged save.
F2No duplicate: no two versions have the same save_id.
F3Reads never fail. Each read of the journal on both instances has the answer 200.
F4During the gap, each save fails with 503 and a retryable error within 10 s. No answer is 500, and no request hangs.
F5The first acknowledged save comes at most 15 s after the new leader, and at most 30 s after step 5.
F6Within 2 s after the end of the load, both instances list the same etag for each uid.
F7The old leader, now a replica, holds the same history rows as the new primary.
F8/readyz gives 200 on both instances during the whole test.
F9No Hune container restarted (restart count 0).

16.12 ProcurAuth series U

The procurauth job runs the runner access-conformance of the ProcurAuth repository with the role pep and the series U.

Section 11.32 owns the run: the fixture, the configuration tests/conformance/config.hcl, the profile tests/conformance/profile.json (service id, operations, skip conditions) and the expected counts. This paragraph gives the CI side only.

  • The job fetches the public repository ProcurAuth/procurauth at one full commit: the commit of the git dependency procurauth-pep (11.10, 17.9). docs/CONFORMANCE.md of Hune names it.
  • The script tests/conformance/run.sh builds the runner and the static binary of Hune in Docker.
  • It starts Hune in a container with --network none and a read-only root. A ClickHouse container joins the network namespace of Hune and listens on 127.0.0.1:8123. At its first start it runs tests/fixtures/clickhouse/init.sql (16.8): the profile and the user of 8.12. The configuration names that user, with password_file.
  • The runner runs in the same network namespace. It is the client, the edge and the PDP on 127.0.0.1:8181. As the edge, it signs the identity tokens with the test keys of ProcurAuth. Hune never reaches a real PDP.
  • At the commit of 19.28, the run gives the counts of 11.32: 110 cases, 61 passed, 0 failed and 49 skipped.

The file tests/conformance/expected.txt lists the known failed cases, one id on each line with its reason. The script exits with 0 only when the set of failed cases equals this list. At the exit of M6, the list MUST be empty, or each line MUST have a written reason. docs/CONFORMANCE.md records the counts of passed, failed and skipped cases.

The unit tests of hune-core compare the expander with the committed vectors, with no Grafana.

The import job makes the vectors again from Grafana. The compose project tests/compose/grafana.yaml runs a pinned Grafana image with the ClickHouse plugin 4.5.1, and a ClickHouse with synthetic data. The plugin archive has a SHA-256 in tests/images.lock. The line holds the download URL of the linux/amd64 archive, because grafana.com refuses a download with no os and arch. The capture tool of 15.14a reads executedQueryString for each synthetic dashboard. The job fails when a vector differs from the committed file.

16.14 Restore drill

The store job runs the drill on each change. It proves that hune export and hune import (5.38, 5.39) lose nothing.

  1. Start from the synthetic seed volume: 20 dashboards in 3 folders, with saves, one move, one delete and one restore in their history. One dashboard also has a version of kind external without bytes: a hook wrote an invalid file, and a save replaced it (5.14). The drill checks that export, import and copy-store keep this version, without bytes.
  2. hune export -c volume.hcl --to /out/a.
  3. hune migrate-store -c migrate.hcl (owner role) on an empty database, then the grants of 5.25. Then hune import -c postgres.hcl --from /out/a (application role).
  4. hune export -c postgres.hcl --to /out/b.
  5. diff -r /out/a /out/b finds no difference.
  6. Do the same in the other direction with hune copy-store --from postgres.hcl --to volume2.hcl (5.37), into a new volume.
  7. hune restore -c <file> --uid <uid> (5.40) of the deleted dashboard on each store gives the document again.

Pass criteria: no difference in step 5 and step 6; each etag is equal on the two stores; serve on each restored store gives the same answer to GET /api/dashboards.

16.15 Image and deployment tests

CaseCheck
G01readelf -l of each binary shows no program interpreter.
G02hune version runs on an amd64 runner and on an arm64 runner, each with its own binary.
G03The image of 17.5 builds locally with no push.
G04docker run --read-only --user 65532:65532 --network none -v "$PWD/deploy/compose/volume/config.hcl:/etc/hune/config.hcl:ro" -v "$PWD/tests/fixtures/secrets:/run/secrets:ro" <image> check-config -c /etc/hune/config.hcl exits 0. The directory tests/fixtures/secrets/ holds test secret files with the names of the example.
G05The image runs serve with the volume example. The health command exits 0 within 10 s, and Docker reports healthy.
G06SIGTERM to the container: exit 0 within server.shutdown_grace.
G07Each compose example of 17.6 and 17.7: docker compose up --wait gives healthy services. GET /api/dashboards through the published port gives 200. docker compose down -v removes everything.

A file with an unknown key, such as a key that 13.8 to 13.12 do not hold, fails the case.

16.16 Fuzz and load tests

Fuzz tests run from M7. tests/images.lock has no image with a nightly toolchain, because the tag rust:nightly-trixie does not exist (2026-11-22). Thus the nightly job prints a note and runs no fuzz target. A person runs the targets with tests/ci/fuzz.sh or tests/ci/nightly.sh --local, on a host with the nightly toolchain.

The targets of fuzz/ are lexer, lexer_pg, expand, document_parse, hcl_config, grafana_import and frame_decode. Each target runs 10 minutes, with a corpus from the fixtures. A crash fails the job, and its input becomes a regression test.

The target lexer_pg reads the lexer and the statement check of the dialect postgres (7.20, 7.25). Bit 3 of the flags byte of expand selects the dialect postgres. With bit 3 clear, the dialect is clickhouse.

The front-end tool ast_fuzz.mjs (16.10a) tests the tree of the query editor. It runs in Node. A person runs it by hand: the job nightly does not run it yet. It is not a target of fuzz/. A crash or a property that fails fails the job, and its text becomes a vector of tests/fixtures/ast/.

The load test runs before 1.0.0 and before each minor release, in the manual job load. The tool is k6 in a pinned image, with the script tests/load/dashboard.js.

  • 52 viewers, a number of browsers that was measured on a Grafana instance that queries ClickHouse, each on the synthetic 45-panel dashboard.
  • A refresh every 30 s for 30 minutes. Each viewer has at most 6 open queries, as the browser.
  • Each panel query reads SELECT number FROM numbers(100), so the time of ClickHouse is small and stable.

Pass criteria:

  • No answer is an error.
  • The p95 of the panel query time is at most 50 ms above the p95 of the same query sent straight to ClickHouse.
  • The resident memory of the process grows by less than 5 percent in the last 20 minutes.

16.17 CI jobs

The workflows are .github/workflows/ci.yaml (each push and pull request), nightly.yaml, conformance.yml and release.yaml (17.3). Each job calls one script of tests/ci/. Each script uses Docker only.

The scripts tests/ci/all.sh and tests/ci/release.sh are the release path of the project. They run every job and the release with no GitHub Actions and no GitHub billing. The workflows in .github/workflows are an option. They run the same jobs on GitHub.

The script tests/ci/all.sh runs the jobs of the merge gate at the same time: policy, rust, store, pgsource, mongosource, api, source, frontend, browser, import, image, procurauth and failover. A full run takes about as long as its longest chain of jobs, and not the sum of the jobs. 16.17a gives the order, the options and the isolation of the jobs.

The script runs procurauth and failover on each full run, with no path filter. The job failover is a stub until M5. The jobs nightly and load stay by hand.

A full run of all.sh on a clean work tree writes the commit in target/ci-all.ok, after all jobs passed. The script tests/ci/release.sh (17.3) refuses a commit that this file does not name.

JobWhenCommandMain steps
policypushtests/ci/policy.shThe checks of 16.18.
rustpushtests/ci/rust.shcargo fmt --all --check; cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings; cargo test --workspace --locked (units, seeds 1 to 64, configuration).
storepushtests/ci/store.shdocker compose -f tests/compose/store.yaml up --abort-on-container-exit --exit-code-from store-tests: cargo test -p hune-store --features conformance --locked on a tmpfs volume, a tmpfs of 1 MiB, PostgreSQL 15 and 18; then the restore drill.
apipushtests/ci/api.shThe compose project of 16.8 and its runs: cargo test -p hune --test api --locked -- --test-threads=1. Then the environment test K14 (13.24) in the same project.
sourcepushtests/ci/source.shThe cases of 16.9 on the two ClickHouse versions.
pgsourcepushtests/ci/pgsource.shThe cases of 16.9a on PostgreSQL 15 and 18, with the compose project tests/compose/pgsource.yaml. The job also runs the ignored tests pg_real of the binary hune, which hold the cases A47, A50 and A51.
mongosourcepushtests/ci/mongosource.shThe cases of 16.9b on MongoDB 7.0 and 8.x, with the compose project tests/compose/mongosource.yaml. A build with no mongo-driver feature runs only the cases of the first part.
frontendpushtests/ci/frontend.shBuild the browser test image; node --test tests/frontend/ in it (16.10a).
browserpushtests/ci/browser.shBuild the test image; npx playwright test in it.
importpushtests/ci/import.shcargo test -p hune-grafana --locked; the CLI cases of 16.13 on the release binary; the expansion vectors from tests/compose/grafana.yaml. The job runs the service import-tests with docker compose run (ci_compose_exec), because the service plugin-fetch ends early with the code 0. A time limit of 25 minutes ends the run.
imagepushtests/ci/image.shThe musl builds of 17.2 on an amd64 and an arm64 runner; the cases of 16.15; the case I14.
procurauthpull request that changes crates/hune/src/access/, the configuration code (crates/hune/src/config/, crates/hclconf/), tests/conformance/ or the ProcurAuth commit (11.32); nightly; releasetests/conformance/run.sh16.12.
failoverpull request that changes crates/hune-store/; nightly; releasetests/failover/run.sh kill then tests/failover/run.sh partition16.11.
nightlyeach nighttests/ci/nightly.sh1,000 random seeds; the fuzz targets only with --local (16.16); the source, pgsource and mongosource jobs on both versions again. The job pgsource runs after the job source.
loadmanualtests/load/run.sh16.16.

A pull request merges only when the jobs of the merge gate pass. The gate grows with the milestones of section 18, because a job can only pass when its code exists:

From the exit ofThe merge gate runsGate cases of store and api
M1policy, rust, image; api on the volume in mode openapi: A01, A02, A33
M2also sourceapi: also A18 to A24 and K14a
M3aalso importno change
M3balso frontend and browserno change
M4also store on the volumestore: the common tests of 5.34 except SC-44 and SC-47, the volume tests of 5.35, SX-01; api: A01 to A38 and A46 on the volume, with and without a primary store
M5store and api also on PostgreSQL 15 and 18; failover when its paths changedstore: every case, the restore drill included; api: A01 to A38 and A46 on both backends
M6api also in mode authzen; procurauth when its paths changedapi: also A39 to A45 and K14b
M7no new jobapi: also K14c, with the OTLP exporter
MPGalso pgsourceapi: also A47 to A51
MMGalso mongosourceapi: also A52 to A56

The file tests/ci/gate-cases.txt holds this table as data: one line <milestone> <job> <case ids> for each cell. The file tests/ci/milestone holds the last milestone that passed, for example M2. The jobs store and api run every case on each push. They fail only when a case of the gate list of that milestone, or of an earlier one, fails. A job or a case that the gate does not run yet gives a result for information only. A person can run the whole gate with tests/ci/all.sh.

16.17a The parallel run of the gate

The script tests/ci/all.sh starts the jobs as background processes. It does not stop at the first failure. Each job runs to its end, so one run shows each failure. The exit code is 1 when a job fails.

OptionEffect
--listPrint the jobs and exit.
--only JOB[,JOB]Run only these jobs, and the build tasks that they need. A run with --only does not record the gate.
--from JOBRun JOB and the jobs after it in the order of --list. A run with --from does not record the gate, except --from policy.
--max-jobs NStart at most N tasks at the same time. The default has no limit. Use it on a host that is smaller than the host of this section.

Some jobs wait for a build task. Each task runs one time, and its result serves each job that needs it. A job that needs a failed task does not start. The other jobs still run.

TaskWhat it doesJobs that wait for it
setupBuild the builder image of 17.2 when it does not exist.build-debug, build-binary, import
build-debugcargo test --workspace --locked --no-run in the builder: the debug artifacts of the test profile.policy, rust, frontend
build-binaryFetch the sources of Cargo.lock one time, then build the release musl binary one time (17.2).store, image, procurauth, frontend; api and browser when their compose files exist

The job frontend waits for the two long builds and for the other jobs of the run, except image. Its timing tests need a quiet host. The jobs pgsource, mongosource and source run cargo on the host and wait for no task. The jobs pgsource and mongosource build their tests in the background while their containers start. They also run their two versions at the same time. Each version has its own compose project, ports and output.

Each job and each task writes its output to target/ci-logs/<name>.log. The script prints one line when a task starts and one line when it ends. It prints one line each minute that names the tasks that run. At the end it prints a table with the duration of each task and the total. It also shows the failed lines and the last 40 lines of the log of each failed task.

Isolation of the jobs. Each job names its containers, networks, volumes, images and folders with its own process id. No job uses a fixed port of the host. A job that publishes a port takes a free port of 127.0.0.1 at its start, with ci_free_port of tests/ci/ci-lib.sh. The function takes a port from 20000 to 29999, which is below the ephemeral ports of Linux. It reserves the port in target/ci-ports/, so two jobs never take the same port. The default mode of procurauth and the restore drill publish no port of the host.

Cargo folders. Each group of jobs has its own target folder, so the jobs do not wait for the build lock of one another. The folder target serves build-debug and rust. The folders target/policy, target/import and target/host serve policy, import, and the three jobs that run cargo on the host. The host builds cannot share artifacts with the builder builds, because the paths of the registry differ. The release binary is in target/<triple>/release/.

The vendor step runs one time. It does nothing when Cargo.lock has not changed since its last run. A second vendor run writes each source file again, and cargo then builds the git dependencies and hune again, which takes more than 3 minutes. A new commit also builds the release binary again, because the binary holds the commit and its date (17.2).

Resources. The full gate needs Docker. On a host with 22 cores and 93 GB of memory, a full run with a cold cache takes about 11 minutes. A host with fewer cores takes longer, and --max-jobs limits the load. The target/ folder of a run takes about 25 GB of disk: target/debug takes 13 GB and target/host takes 6 GB. Make sure that 40 GB of disk are free.

The jobs that are off the longest chain run with a low CPU weight (nice and --cpu-shares). The longest chain is the release build and the jobs that use its binary.

The seeded random tests of hune-core (16.4) run their seeds on all cores of the host, with one thread for each seed at a time. Each seed has its own generator, so a seed gives the same cases as in a run on one thread. The seeds are still 1 to 64, with the same count of cases. The first failure stops the other threads after their current seed. Its report names the seed.

16.18 Policy checks

The policy job runs these checks. Each check fails the job when its command finds a match or a difference. The patterns are split with '' where they would match the policy script itself.

CheckCommand
No image pushgrep -rnE --exclude=policy.sh 'ghcr''\.io\|docker/login''-action\|packages: *wr''ite\|push: *tr''ue' .github/workflows tests/ci tests/compose tests/conformance build tools deploy prints nothing. A second check does the same for docker push, docker login, buildx --push and imagetools create.
No environment read outside the engine (3.4, R2)grep -rnE --include='*.rs' '(^\|[^[:alnum:]_])env::(var\|var_os\|vars\|vars_os\|set_var\|remove_var)\b' crates \| grep -vE '^crates/hclconf/src/env\.rs:\|/build\.rs:\|/tests/' prints nothing. Only crates/hclconf/src/env.rs is exempt; tests and build.rs are exempt too.

It skips comment lines. A finding is accepted only when the list tests/ci/env-read-allow.txt holds it. The list is empty.

The list is outside the repository: the CI secret HUNE_POLICY_WORDS, or the untracked file private/policy-words.txt. With no list, both checks fail.
