Documentation languagepython3 -I tools/ste_lint.py docs/*.md README.md CHANGELOG.md reports no error

The exemption /tests/ also covers the files named tests.rs. This applies to the three checks for the MongoDB driver: the command document, the MongoDB crate and the write call. The first pattern is a method call, because run_command is also the name of a function of the CLI. The crate name mongodb:: or bson:: counts only at the start of a path: config::mongodb::block is a module of Hune.

16.19 Manual checks before 1.0.0

  • The CloudNativePG drill. Run two Hune instances of the example of 17.8 under load on a K3s cluster with CloudNativePG. Run a failover drill. Pass: the criteria F1 to F3, F8 and F9 of 16.11. Saves fail with 503 during the gap of 1 to 2 minutes.
  • The load test of 16.16.
  • The verification of 15.14, when a dashboard changed after the cutover verification.
  • The cases B170 and B171 in Firefox. WebDriver BiDi cannot run them (16.10). Run them by hand. Pass: the same results as in Chromium.
  • The whole browser suite in WebKit. WebKit has no driver on the test host. Run the cases by hand in MiniBrowser or in Safari. Pass: the same results as in Chromium.
  • The input checks of B170 and B171 in Firefox and WebKit. Make a real IME composition and a real dead key (for example ´, then e). Each of them must end the folded view before the composition starts. Use a layout where the key BracketLeft gives another character. Ctrl+Shift+Bracket must still fold.

17. Delivery

17.1 Repository layout

The repository is ProcurAuth/hune (https://github.com/ProcurAuth/hune). The project licence is MIT OR Apache-2.0. The files LICENSE-MIT and LICENSE-APACHE hold the texts. The project depends on the repository https://github.com/ProcurAuth/procurauth.

text
hune/
  Cargo.toml                 workspace; [workspace.dependencies] with exact pins (17.9)
  Cargo.lock                 committed; every build uses --locked
  vendor/mongodb/            the local copy of the crate mongodb 3.10.0 with no environment read (8b.2); vendor/mongodb.patch,
                             vendor/mongodb.lock.json
  rust-toolchain.toml        one exact stable version, the two musl targets, rustfmt, clippy
  deny.toml                  cargo deny policy (17.9)
  deps.lock.txt              the resolved crate list (17.9)
  CHANGELOG.md  README.md  LICENSE-MIT  LICENSE-APACHE  LICENSE-THIRD-PARTY
  crates/
    hune-core/              format, limits, ids, canonical JSON, etag, lexer, macros, variables, frames; no I/O
    hclconf/                 HCL engine of Hune, with PROVENANCE; edition = "2021" (below)
    hune-store/             Store trait, volume, PostgreSQL, conformance suite (feature "conformance")
    hune-source/            DataSource trait, ClickHouse HTTP client, frames, limits
    hune-grafana/           the importer (pure functions)
    hune/                   the binary: build.rs, src/main.rs, src/config/ (key table), src/cli/ (output.rs),
                             src/server/, src/access/, tests/ (api, failover)
  web/                       the front end of 9.2; web/js/vendor/chdash/ and web/css/vendor/chdash/ hold the copied engine
  docs/                      this specification, the configuration reference, ACCESS.md, CONFORMANCE.md
  deploy/
    compose/volume/          compose.yaml, config.hcl (17.6)
    compose/postgres/        compose.yaml, config.hcl (17.7)
    kubernetes/              example manifests (17.8)
  build/
    Dockerfile.build         the builder image (17.2)
    Dockerfile               the FROM scratch image (17.5)
  tests/
    images.lock              every test image and archive, with its digest
    ci/                      one script for each CI job, `all.sh` (the merge gate) and `release.sh` (16.17, 17.3)
    compose/                 the compose projects of the test layers
    fixtures/                synthetic data: grafana/, dashboards/, clickhouse/, sql-lex-vectors.json
    golden/                  expansion vectors, reports, documents
    browser/  conformance/  failover/  load/
  tools/
    ste_lint.py              the language check of 16.18
    parity/                  the verification tools of 15.14 (scripts only, no data)
    check-vendor-lock.sh  check-addresses.sh  changelog-section.sh  third-party.sh  update-chdash.sh
  fuzz/                      cargo-fuzz targets (16.16)
  .github/workflows/         ci.yaml, nightly.yaml, conformance.yml, release.yaml

The .gitignore file holds /target/, /dist/, /private/ and /parity-out/. It does not hold /vendor/: the directory holds the local copy of mongodb.

The crate hclconf pins edition = "2021" in its Cargo.toml, and its PROVENANCE file records it. Reason: its file src/env.rs calls std::env::remove_var, which is an unsafe function from the edition 2024. Each crate of the workspace forbids unsafe code (3.4, R5). The other crates take the edition of the workspace. The environment rule of 3.4 (R2) and the check of 16.18 exempt crates/hclconf/src/env.rs only.

17.2 Build

The build gives one static binary for each target: x86_64-unknown-linux-musl and aarch64-unknown-linux-musl. Each target builds on a runner of its own architecture: ubuntu-24.04 for amd64 and ubuntu-24.04-arm for arm64. So no build crosses an architecture.

The builder image comes from build/Dockerfile.build. Its base is the official rust image of the version of rust-toolchain.toml, pinned by a multi-architecture digest in tests/images.lock. It adds musl-tools and jq from Debian, and cargo-deny at the version of tests/images.lock.

dockerfile
ARG RUST_IMAGE
FROM ${RUST_IMAGE}
ARG CARGO_DENY_VERSION
RUN apt-get update \
 && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends musl-tools jq \
 && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*
RUN cargo install --locked "cargo-deny@${CARGO_DENY_VERSION}"

The build has two stages. The first stage has network access and fetches the sources of every crate. The second stage has no network and builds.

sh
# Stage 1, with network: fetch the sources of every crate of Cargo.lock. The directory vendor/ of the
# repository holds the local copy of mongodb, so the fetched sources go to target/cargo-vendor/.
docker run --rm -v "$PWD:/src" -w /src hune-builder \
  sh -c 'cargo vendor --locked --versioned-dirs target/cargo-vendor > vendor-config.toml'

# Stage 2, without network: build the static binary.
TRIPLE=x86_64-unknown-linux-musl          # aarch64-unknown-linux-musl on the arm64 runner
docker run --rm --network none -v "$PWD:/src" -w /src \
  -e SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH="$(git log -1 --format=%ct)" \
  -e HUNE_BUILD_COMMIT="$(git rev-parse HEAD)" \
  -e CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0 \
  -e "CC_x86_64_unknown_linux_musl=musl-gcc" -e "CC_aarch64_unknown_linux_musl=musl-gcc" \
  -e RUSTFLAGS="--remap-path-prefix=/src=/hune --remap-path-prefix=/usr/local/cargo=/cargo" \
  hune-builder \
  cargo build --release --locked --offline --config vendor-config.toml --target "$TRIPLE" -p hune

The release profile of Cargo.toml:

toml
[profile.release]
opt-level = 3
lto = "fat"
codegen-units = 1
strip = "symbols"
debug = false
incremental = false

build.rs reads HUNE_BUILD_COMMIT at build time. hune version prints hune <version> <commit of 12 characters> <target>. The same sources, toolchain and builder image give the same bytes. The release job checks it (17.3).

17.3 Release workflow

A person starts the workflow release.yaml by hand (workflow_dispatch) on main with two inputs: version (for example 0.1.0) and arm64, a boolean.

  1. Check the input. It matches ^[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+$. It equals the version of the workspace in Cargo.toml. CHANGELOG.md has the heading ## <version> - <YYYY-MM-DD>. The tag v<version> does not exist.
  2. Run the jobs policy, rust, store, api, source, browser, import, image, procurauth and failover on the commit. All must pass.
  3. Build each target two times, in two new containers (17.2). The two binaries of a target must have the same SHA-256.
  4. Check each binary: no program interpreter (readelf -l), and hune version prints the version and the commit.
  5. Make the archives (two, or one when arm64 is false) and SHA256SUMS (17.4).
  6. Make a draft release v<version> with the title Hune v<version>, the target commit, the archives and SHA256SUMS. The notes are the version section of CHANGELOG.md (tools/changelog-section.sh). GitHub creates no tag for a draft.
  7. Check the draft: it has exactly these files (three, or two when arm64 is false); download them; sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS passes.
  8. Publish the draft with one call. The publish creates the tag.
  9. When the job fails before the publish call starts, delete the draft. After the publish call starts, never delete the draft or the release. A person then checks the release and the tag by hand.

The release always has an amd64 archive (a static musl binary). The workflow builds the arm64 archive when arm64 is true. With arm64 set to false, it builds the amd64 archive only. Use this value when the repository has no arm64 runner. SHA256SUMS and the draft then hold one archive, and step 7 expects two files.

The workflow gives the value to the CI, and its job image also runs on amd64 only. With arm64 set to true, the workflow builds and checks both architectures. ARCHIVES below holds the names of the archives.

sh
gh release create "v$V" --draft --target "$GITHUB_SHA" --title "Hune v$V" --notes-file notes.md \
  $ARCHIVES SHA256SUMS
gh release download "v$V" --dir check
(cd check && sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS)
gh release edit "v$V" --draft=false --latest

The release job has the permission contents: write only. No workflow has packages: write, logs in to a registry or pushes an image (16.18).

Release with no GitHub Actions

The scripts tests/ci/all.sh and tests/ci/release.sh are the release path of the project. They run every job and the release with no GitHub Actions and no GitHub billing. The workflows in .github/workflows are an option. They run the same jobs on GitHub.

The operator makes a release by hand, on main, with these steps:

  1. Run tests/ci/all.sh on the commit. Every job of 16.17 must pass. A full run on a clean work tree writes the commit in target/ci-all.ok.
  2. Run tests/ci/release-build.sh <version> on a machine of each architecture. It does the steps 3 to 5 above and makes the archive in dist/.
  3. Copy the archive of the other architecture into dist/ of the machine that makes the release.
  4. Push main. The target commit of the release must be on origin.
  5. Run tests/ci/release.sh <version>. Add --amd64-only when no arm64 machine exists.

The script tests/ci/release.sh does the steps 1 and 5 to 9 above, with gh that the operator has logged in. It checks that main and origin/main are at the commit, that the work tree is clean, and that target/ci-all.ok names the commit. It makes dist/SHA256SUMS and the notes (tools/changelog-section.sh). It makes the draft, checks it, and publishes it with one call. It deletes the draft when a step fails before the publish call. After that call it never deletes anything.

The option --partial-gate "<text>" of tests/ci/release.sh replaces the check of target/ci-all.ok. Use it only when a partial gate is enough for the release. The text MUST NOT be empty, and the script refuses a text of blanks.

The text names each job that ran on the commit and each job that did not run. It also says that GitHub Actions did not run. The script prints the text, and it writes the text in the release notes under the heading "Checks". All other checks of step 1 stay. The script never writes target/ci-all.ok itself.

17.4 Artefacts and SHA256SUMS

FileContent
hune_<version>_linux_amd64.tar.gzThe directory hune_<version>_linux_amd64/ (below).
hune_<version>_linux_arm64.tar.gzThe directory hune_<version>_linux_arm64/. The release has it only when the arm64 build runs.
SHA256SUMSOne line for each archive: the SHA-256 in hex, two spaces, the file name. The output of sha256sum.

Each archive holds one directory with these files:

FileContent
huneThe static binary, mode 0755.
DockerfileThe image file of 17.5.
config.reference.hclThe output of hune print-reference.
deploy/The examples of 17.6, 17.7 and 17.8.
LICENSE-MITThe text of the MIT licence. The project licence is MIT OR Apache-2.0.
LICENSE-APACHEThe text of the Apache-2.0 licence.
LICENSE-THIRD-PARTYThe licence notice of each crate of deps.lock.txt, with the local copy of the crate mongodb (Apache-2.0). It also holds the notice of the copied chart engine (MIT, ClickHouse Dash), of the font Inter (SIL OFL 1.1) and of hclconf (tools/third-party.sh). These parts keep their own licences.
README.md, CHANGELOG.mdThe files of the repository.

The archives are deterministic:

sh
tar --sort=name --mtime="@${SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH}" --owner=0 --group=0 --numeric-owner --format=gnu \
  -cf - "hune_${V}_linux_${ARCH}" | gzip -9n > "hune_${V}_linux_${ARCH}.tar.gz"
sha256sum "hune_${V}_linux_amd64.tar.gz" "hune_${V}_linux_arm64.tar.gz" > SHA256SUMS

An operator checks a download with sha256sum -c --ignore-missing SHA256SUMS.

17.5 The image

The image is FROM scratch and holds the binary only. The operator builds it locally from the release archive, or the CI builds it for a test. The project publishes no container image. Nobody pushes it to a registry: not GHCR, not Docker Hub, not any other registry. The release archive holds the Dockerfile for a local build.

dockerfile
# Build locally in the extracted release archive: docker build -t hune:<version> .
# Never push this image to a registry.
FROM scratch
COPY --chmod=0555 hune /hune
USER 65532:65532
ENTRYPOINT ["/hune"]
CMD ["serve", "-c", "/etc/hune/config.hcl"]
HEALTHCHECK --interval=10s --timeout=3s --start-period=20s --retries=3 \
  CMD ["/hune", "health", "-c", "/etc/hune/config.hcl"]

The contract of the image:

  • The process is PID 1 and handles SIGTERM itself (section 14). There is no shell and no init.
  • The image has no CA store. Each TLS link names its CA file in the configuration.
  • The image has no writable path. Hune writes only to the volume path of the configuration, which the operator mounts.
  • The configuration mounts at /etc/hune/config.hcl. Secret files mount under /run/secrets/ or another path that the configuration names.
  • hune health is the health command, because the image has no curl (section 14).

17.6 Compose example: volume

This example is the LOCAL use case: one instance, a mounted folder of dashboards, mode open. Mode open has no authentication, so the example publishes the main port on 127.0.0.1 only. It does not publish the ops port (12.6): the health check runs inside the container. A real deployment puts a proxy that checks the login in front of Hune, for example with the auth_request module of nginx (15.15).

Prepare the directory once:

sh
install -d -m 0750 -o 65532 -g 65532 dashboards
install -d -m 0700 secrets
install -m 0400 -o 65532 -g 65532 /path/to/clickhouse_password secrets/clickhouse_password
install -m 0444 /path/to/clickhouse_ca.pem secrets/clickhouse_ca.pem
docker build -t hune:0.2.0 /path/to/hune_0.2.0_linux_amd64

deploy/compose/volume/compose.yaml:

yaml
# Hune with a mounted folder of dashboards: one instance, mode open.
# The image comes from a local build of the release archive. It is never pulled or pushed.
name: hune-volume
services:
  hune:
    image: hune:0.2.0
    pull_policy: never
    user: "65532:65532"
    read_only: true
    cap_drop: [ALL]
    security_opt: ["no-new-privileges:true"]
    command: ["serve", "-c", "/etc/hune/config.hcl"]
    volumes:
      - ./config.hcl:/etc/hune/config.hcl:ro
      - ./dashboards:/var/lib/hune/dashboards
    secrets:
      - source: clickhouse_password
        target: clickhouse_password
      - source: clickhouse_ca
        target: clickhouse_ca.pem
    ports:
      - "127.0.0.1:8080:8080"
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "/hune", "health", "-c", "/etc/hune/config.hcl"]
      interval: 10s
      timeout: 3s
      start_period: 20s
      retries: 3
    stop_grace_period: 30s
    restart: unless-stopped
secrets:
  clickhouse_password:
    file: ./secrets/clickhouse_password
  clickhouse_ca:
    file: ./secrets/clickhouse_ca.pem

deploy/compose/volume/config.hcl starts from the file of 13.16. It changes the secret paths, the ClickHouse host, shutdown_grace, max_request_bytes and queue_wait. It has each key of 13.8 to 13.12a that applies, and no other key (G09):

hcl
server {
  listen                  = "0.0.0.0:8080"
  ops_listen              = "0.0.0.0:9090"
  base_path               = "/"
  max_connections         = 1024
  idle_timeout            = "75s"
  shutdown_grace          = "20s"
  max_request_bytes       = 4259840
  max_result_memory_bytes = 536870912
}

log {
  level = "info"
}

telemetry {
  enabled = false
}

storage {
  volume "main" {
    path            = "/var/lib/hune/dashboards"
    primary         = true
    rescan_interval = "5s"
    history_keep    = 100
  }
}

access {
  mode = "open"
}

clickhouse "prod" {
  label              = "Production"
  url                = "https://clickhouse.example.internal:8443"
  user               = "hune"
  password_file      = "/run/secrets/clickhouse_password"
  database           = "analytics"
  connect_timeout    = "5s"
  max_execution_time = "60s"
  max_result_rows    = 100000
  max_result_bytes   = 33554432
  max_query_size     = 1048576
  max_concurrent     = 16
  queue_wait         = "10s"

  tls {
    ca_file     = "/run/secrets/clickhouse_ca.pem"
    server_name = "clickhouse.example.internal"
  }
}

stop_grace_period (30 s) is server.shutdown_grace (20 s) plus SHUTDOWN_DRAIN (5 s) plus 5 s, the minimum of 14.13. server.idle_timeout (75 s) is above the keep-alive time of 60 s of an nginx upstream, as 12.6 asks. max_request_bytes is the document limit of 4 MiB plus 64 KiB, the lowest value of 13.8. The mode open forbids max_concurrent_per_subject (13.14, C13).

At a deployment behind an nginx that exists, the same file differs in these values. The compose service joins the network of nginx and publishes no port.

KeyValue
server.base_path"/dashboards/"
primary of the volumeabsent during the parallel run (a read-only store); true from T (15.15)
clickhouse "prod".url"https://<clickhouse host>:8443"
clickhouse "prod".database"<database>"
clickhouse "prod".userthe account of 19.6
clickhouse "prod".max_result_rows, .max_result_bytesthe values of the M0 measure (19.13)

17.7 Compose example: PostgreSQL

This example shows several instances on one PostgreSQL. It is NOT a high-availability setup: the one PostgreSQL is a single point of failure. The reference for high availability is CloudNativePG (17.8). The example uses no TLS to PostgreSQL on the private compose network (sslmode = "disable"), so the start gives the warning W5 of 13.15.

The example uses the two roles of 5.25. serve stops at start with a role that is a superuser or that owns the tables (5.29, step 6). The superuser postgres creates the roles at the first start of the database. The owner role hune_owner runs migrate-store. The service grant then gives the rights of 5.25 to the application role hune_app.

Prepare the directory as in 17.6, with three more secret files: secrets/pg_super_password, secrets/pg_owner_password and secrets/pg_app_password. The user 999 of the image postgres and the user 65532 of Hune read them. So these three files have the mode 0444. The directory secrets/ has the mode 0700 (install -d -m 0700 secrets, 17.6), so that no other user of the host reads them. The service migrate mounts only pg_owner_password, because migrate-store reads only the values of the block storage (13.18).

deploy/compose/postgres/compose.yaml:

yaml
# Hune with PostgreSQL: two instances, one PostgreSQL and a small load balancer.
# NOT high availability: the one PostgreSQL is a single point of failure.
name: hune-postgres
services:
  postgres:
    image: postgres:18.4-trixie
    environment:
      POSTGRES_DB: hune
      POSTGRES_USER: postgres
      POSTGRES_PASSWORD_FILE: /run/secrets/pg_super_password
    secrets: [pg_super_password, pg_owner_password, pg_app_password]
    configs:
      - source: roles_sh
        target: /docker-entrypoint-initdb.d/10-roles.sh
    volumes:
      - pgdata:/var/lib/postgresql
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "pg_isready", "-U", "postgres", "-d", "hune"]
      interval: 5s
      timeout: 3s
      retries: 20
    restart: unless-stopped

  migrate:
    image: hune:0.2.0
    pull_policy: never
    user: "65532:65532"
    read_only: true
    cap_drop: [ALL]
    command: ["migrate-store", "-c", "/etc/hune/migrate.hcl"]
    volumes:
      - ./migrate.hcl:/etc/hune/migrate.hcl:ro
    secrets:
      - source: pg_owner_password
        target: pg_owner_password
    depends_on:
      postgres:
        condition: service_healthy
    restart: "no"

  grant:
    image: postgres:18.4-trixie
    entrypoint: ["sh", "-c"]
    command:
      - >-
        PGPASSWORD="$$(cat /run/secrets/pg_super_password)"
        psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -h postgres -U postgres -d hune
        -c "GRANT SELECT ON hune_schema TO hune_app"
        -c "GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON hune_folders, hune_dashboards, hune_versions TO hune_app"
    secrets: [pg_super_password]
    depends_on:
      migrate:
        condition: service_completed_successfully
    restart: "no"

  hune-a: &hune
    image: hune:0.2.0
    pull_policy: never
    user: "65532:65532"
    read_only: true
    cap_drop: [ALL]
    security_opt: ["no-new-privileges:true"]
    command: ["serve", "-c", "/etc/hune/config.hcl"]
    volumes:
      - ./config.hcl:/etc/hune/config.hcl:ro
    secrets:
      - source: pg_app_password
        target: pg_app_password
      - source: clickhouse_password
        target: clickhouse_password
      - source: clickhouse_ca
        target: clickhouse_ca.pem
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "/hune", "health", "-c", "/etc/hune/config.hcl"]
      interval: 10s
      timeout: 3s
      start_period: 20s
      retries: 3
    stop_grace_period: 30s
    depends_on:
      grant:
        condition: service_completed_successfully
    restart: unless-stopped

  hune-b: *hune

  lb:
    image: nginx:1.28-alpine
    configs:
      - source: lb_conf
        target: /etc/nginx/conf.d/default.conf
    ports:
      - "127.0.0.1:8080:8080"
    depends_on:
      hune-a:
        condition: service_healthy
      hune-b:
        condition: service_healthy
    restart: unless-stopped

configs:
  roles_sh:
    content: |
      #!/bin/sh
      set -eu
      psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 --username postgres --dbname hune \
        -v owner_pw="$$(cat /run/secrets/pg_owner_password)" \
        -v app_pw="$$(cat /run/secrets/pg_app_password)" <<'SQL'
      CREATE ROLE hune_owner LOGIN PASSWORD :'owner_pw';
      CREATE ROLE hune_app LOGIN PASSWORD :'app_pw';
      GRANT CONNECT ON DATABASE hune TO hune_owner, hune_app;
      GRANT CREATE, USAGE ON SCHEMA public TO hune_owner;
      GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA public TO hune_app;
      SQL
  lb_conf:
    content: |
      upstream hune {
        server hune-a:8080;
        server hune-b:8080;
      }
      server {
        listen 8080;
        location / {
          proxy_pass http://hune;
          proxy_set_header Host $$http_host;
          proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-For $$proxy_add_x_forwarded_for;
          proxy_read_timeout 120s;
        }
      }

secrets:
  pg_super_password:
    file: ./secrets/pg_super_password
  pg_owner_password:
    file: ./secrets/pg_owner_password
  pg_app_password:
    file: ./secrets/pg_app_password
  clickhouse_password:
    file: ./secrets/clickhouse_password
  clickhouse_ca:
    file: ./secrets/clickhouse_ca.pem

volumes:
  pgdata: {}

$$ is the compose form of one $. The load balancer sends $http_host, which keeps the port of the browser, so the Origin check of 12.7 passes. The image postgres runs the script 10-roles.sh only at the first start of an empty data volume. deploy/compose/postgres/config.hcl is the file of 17.6 with this storage block:

hcl
storage {
  postgres {
    host              = "postgres"
    port              = 5432
    database          = "hune"
    user              = "hune_app"
    password_file     = "/run/secrets/pg_app_password"
    sslmode           = "disable"
    max_connections   = 8
    connect_timeout   = "5s"
    statement_timeout = "10s"
    poll_interval     = "5s"
    primary           = true
    history_keep      = 100
  }
}

deploy/compose/postgres/migrate.hcl is a complete file too (5.25). It equals config.hcl, except two keys of storage.postgres: user = "hune_owner" and password_file = "/run/secrets/pg_owner_password".

17.8 Kubernetes example manifests

The files of deploy/kubernetes/ are documentation: an example of the DISTRIBUTED use case on K3s. The CI checks them with kubeconform (G08), but no test applies them.

The reference for high availability is a CloudNativePG cluster that the operator already runs. The example uses the cluster postgresql in the namespace data. That cluster has CloudNativePG 1.30, PostgreSQL 18.4, three instances, and a synchronous quorum with dataDurability: required.

The example holds one Deployment, one Job and the CloudNativePG objects, with the ConfigMap and the Service that they need. It holds no PodDisruptionBudget, no NetworkPolicy and no backup job: these depend on the cluster of the operator. The daily hune export of 5.41 is a job of the operator (hune export -c <file> --to <empty dir>, 5.38).

FileContent
10-config.yamlThe ConfigMap hune-config-0-4-0 with two complete files (5.25): config.hcl (application role) and migrate.hcl (owner role). Its name holds the version.
20-migrate-job.yamlThe Job of hune migrate-store for one version, with migrate.hcl. Its name holds the version.
30-deployment.yamlThe Deployment (2 replicas) and the Service hune (ports 8080 and 9090).
cnpg/database.yamlThe CloudNativePG Database resource, in the namespace data.

Each version has its own ConfigMap, because a new version can add, remove or rename keys (13.26). The Deployment of a version names the ConfigMap of the same version. So a rollback to the old Deployment also reads the old file.

The image never comes from a registry. The operator builds it from the release archive and imports it on each node. The Deployment uses imagePullPolicy: Never:

sh
docker build -t hune:0.2.0 /path/to/hune_0.2.0_linux_amd64
docker save hune:0.2.0 | sudo k3s ctr images import -

The secrets never enter a file of the repository. The operator makes them once:

sh
kubectl create namespace hune
for role in owner app; do
  pw="$(openssl rand -base64 30)"
  kubectl -n data create secret generic "hune-${role}-role" --type=kubernetes.io/basic-auth \
    --from-literal=username="hune_${role}" --from-literal=password="$pw"
  kubectl -n hune create secret generic "hune-pg-${role}" --from-literal=password="$pw"
done
kubectl -n data get secret postgresql-ca -o jsonpath='{.data.ca\.crt}' | base64 -d > ca.crt
kubectl -n hune create secret generic hune-pg-ca --from-file=ca.crt
rm ca.crt
kubectl -n hune create secret generic hune-clickhouse \
  --from-file=password=/path/to/clickhouse_password --from-file=ca.crt=/path/to/clickhouse_ca.pem
kubectl -n hune create secret generic hune-edge --from-file=jwks.json=/path/to/edge-jwks.json
kubectl -n hune create secret generic hune-pdp \
  --from-file=token=/path/to/pdp_token --from-file=ca.crt=/path/to/pdp_ca.pem

The two roles of 5.25 go in the spec of the Cluster of the operator. Neither role is a superuser:

yaml
spec:
  managed:
    roles:
      - name: hune_owner
        ensure: present
        login: true
        passwordSecret:
          name: hune-owner-role
      - name: hune_app
        ensure: present
        login: true
        passwordSecret:
          name: hune-app-role

cnpg/database.yaml:

yaml
apiVersion: postgresql.cnpg.io/v1
kind: Database
metadata:
  name: hune
  namespace: data
spec:
  cluster:
    name: postgresql
  name: hune
  owner: hune_owner

The owner of the database owns its schema public, so hune_owner can create the tables of Hune. After the first migrate-store, the operator gives the rights of 5.25 to hune_app, on the primary:

sh
primary="$(kubectl -n data get pod -l cnpg.io/cluster=postgresql,cnpg.io/instanceRole=primary -o name)"
kubectl -n data exec "$primary" -c postgres -- psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -d hune \
  -c "GRANT SELECT ON hune_schema TO hune_app" \
  -c "GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON hune_folders, hune_dashboards, hune_versions TO hune_app"

10-config.yaml holds the mode authzen behind the ProcurAuth edge. Its key config.hcl starts from the file of 13.17, with the telemetry off, and names the application role. Its key migrate.hcl holds the same text, except two lines of storage.postgres: user = "hune_owner" and password_file = "/etc/hune/secrets/pg-owner/password". The file of the repository holds both keys in full. The key config.hcl:

yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: ConfigMap
metadata:
  name: hune-config-0-4-0
  namespace: hune
data:
  config.hcl: |
    server {
      listen                  = "0.0.0.0:8080"
      ops_listen              = "0.0.0.0:9090"
      base_path               = "/"
      max_connections         = 1024
      idle_timeout            = "75s"
      shutdown_grace          = "20s"
      max_request_bytes       = 4259840
      max_result_memory_bytes = 536870912
    }

    log {
      level = "info"
    }

    telemetry {
      enabled = false
    }

    storage {
      postgres {
        host              = "postgresql-rw.data.svc"
        port              = 5432
        database          = "hune"
        user              = "hune_app"
        password_file     = "/etc/hune/secrets/pg-app/password"
        sslmode           = "verify-full"
        max_connections   = 8
        connect_timeout   = "5s"
        statement_timeout = "10s"
        poll_interval     = "5s"
        primary           = true
        history_keep      = 100

        tls {
          ca_file     = "/etc/hune/secrets/pg-ca/ca.crt"
          server_name = "postgresql-rw.data.svc"
        }
      }
    }

    access {
      mode = "authzen"

      identity {
        header           = "X-Identity-Token"
        issuer           = "https://portal.example.internal"
        audience         = "dashboards"
        public_keys_file = "/etc/hune/secrets/edge/jwks.json"
        max_age          = "5m"
        clock_skew       = "30s"
      }

      pdp {
        url             = "https://pdp.procurauth.svc:8443"
        token_file      = "/etc/hune/secrets/pdp/token"
        ca_file         = "/etc/hune/secrets/pdp/ca.crt"
        plain_http      = false
        timeout         = "500ms"
        cache_ttl       = "30s"
        max_connections = 4
      }
    }

    clickhouse "prod" {
      label                      = "Production"
      url                        = "https://clickhouse.example.internal:8443"
      user                       = "hune"
      password_file              = "/etc/hune/secrets/clickhouse/password"
      database                   = "analytics"
      connect_timeout            = "5s"
      max_execution_time         = "60s"
      max_result_rows            = 100000
      max_result_bytes           = 33554432
      max_query_size             = 1048576
      max_concurrent             = 16
      queue_wait                 = "10s"
      max_concurrent_per_subject = 6

      tls {
        ca_file     = "/etc/hune/secrets/clickhouse/ca.crt"
        server_name = "clickhouse.example.internal"
      }
    }

20-migrate-job.yaml (the name holds the version, so each version has its own Job). migrate-store uses only the block storage, so the Job mounts only the PostgreSQL secrets. This needs the rule of 13.18 by which a subcommand reads only the _env and _file values of the blocks that it uses:

yaml
apiVersion: batch/v1
kind: Job
metadata:
  name: hune-migrate-0-4-0
  namespace: hune
spec:
  backoffLimit: 0
  ttlSecondsAfterFinished: 86400
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        app.kubernetes.io/name: hune-migrate
    spec:
      restartPolicy: Never
      automountServiceAccountToken: false
      securityContext:
        runAsNonRoot: true
        runAsUser: 65532
        runAsGroup: 65532
        fsGroup: 65532
        seccompProfile:
          type: RuntimeDefault
      containers:
        - name: migrate
          image: hune:0.2.0
          imagePullPolicy: Never
          args: ["migrate-store", "-c", "/etc/hune/migrate.hcl"]
          securityContext:
            allowPrivilegeEscalation: false
            readOnlyRootFilesystem: true
            capabilities:
              drop: ["ALL"]
          volumeMounts:
            - { name: config, mountPath: /etc/hune/migrate.hcl, subPath: migrate.hcl, readOnly: true }
            - { name: pg-owner, mountPath: /etc/hune/secrets/pg-owner, readOnly: true }
            - { name: pg-ca, mountPath: /etc/hune/secrets/pg-ca, readOnly: true }
      volumes:
        - { name: config, configMap: { name: hune-config-0-4-0 } }
        - { name: pg-owner, secret: { secretName: hune-pg-owner, defaultMode: 0440 } }
        - { name: pg-ca, secret: { secretName: hune-pg-ca, defaultMode: 0440 } }

30-deployment.yaml. terminationGracePeriodSeconds (30 s) is server.shutdown_grace (20 s) plus SHUTDOWN_DRAIN (5 s) plus 5 s, the minimum of 14.13:

yaml
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  name: hune
  namespace: hune
  labels:
    app.kubernetes.io/name: hune
spec:
  replicas: 2
  revisionHistoryLimit: 3
  strategy:
    type: RollingUpdate
    rollingUpdate:
      maxUnavailable: 0
      maxSurge: 1
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      app.kubernetes.io/name: hune
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        app.kubernetes.io/name: hune
      annotations:
        prometheus.io/scrape: "true"
        prometheus.io/port: "9090"
        prometheus.io/path: /metrics
    spec:
      automountServiceAccountToken: false
      terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 30
      securityContext:
        runAsNonRoot: true
        runAsUser: 65532
        runAsGroup: 65532
        fsGroup: 65532
        seccompProfile:
          type: RuntimeDefault
      topologySpreadConstraints:
        - maxSkew: 1
          topologyKey: kubernetes.io/hostname
          whenUnsatisfiable: ScheduleAnyway
          labelSelector:
            matchLabels:
              app.kubernetes.io/name: hune
      containers:
        - name: hune
          image: hune:0.2.0
          imagePullPolicy: Never
          args: ["serve", "-c", "/etc/hune/config.hcl"]
          ports:
            - { name: http, containerPort: 8080 }
            - { name: ops, containerPort: 9090 }
          readinessProbe:
            httpGet: { path: /readyz, port: ops }
            periodSeconds: 5
            failureThreshold: 2
          livenessProbe:
            httpGet: { path: /livez, port: ops }
            periodSeconds: 10
            failureThreshold: 3
          resources:
            requests: { cpu: 100m, memory: 128Mi }
            limits: { memory: 1Gi }
          securityContext:
            allowPrivilegeEscalation: false
            readOnlyRootFilesystem: true
            capabilities:
              drop: ["ALL"]
          volumeMounts:
            - { name: config, mountPath: /etc/hune/config.hcl, subPath: config.hcl, readOnly: true }
            - { name: pg-app, mountPath: /etc/hune/secrets/pg-app, readOnly: true }
            - { name: pg-ca, mountPath: /etc/hune/secrets/pg-ca, readOnly: true }
            - { name: clickhouse, mountPath: /etc/hune/secrets/clickhouse, readOnly: true }
            - { name: edge, mountPath: /etc/hune/secrets/edge, readOnly: true }
            - { name: pdp, mountPath: /etc/hune/secrets/pdp, readOnly: true }
      volumes:
        - { name: config, configMap: { name: hune-config-0-4-0 } }
        - { name: pg-app, secret: { secretName: hune-pg-app, defaultMode: 0440 } }
        - { name: pg-ca, secret: { secretName: hune-pg-ca, defaultMode: 0440 } }
        - { name: clickhouse, secret: { secretName: hune-clickhouse, defaultMode: 0440 } }
        - { name: edge, secret: { secretName: hune-edge, defaultMode: 0440 } }
        - { name: pdp, secret: { secretName: hune-pdp, defaultMode: 0440 } }
---
apiVersion: v1
kind: Service
metadata:
  name: hune
  namespace: hune
spec:
  selector:
    app.kubernetes.io/name: hune
  ports:
    - { name: http, port: 8080, targetPort: http }
    - { name: ops, port: 9090, targetPort: ops }

The order of a rollout:

  1. Build the image and import it on each node.
  2. Make the namespace and the secrets. Apply the two roles, cnpg/database.yaml and 10-config.yaml.
  3. Apply 20-migrate-job.yaml. Wait: kubectl -n hune wait --for=condition=complete --timeout=5m job/hune-migrate-0-4-0.
  4. Give the rights of 5.25 to hune_app (the psql command above).
  5. Apply 30-deployment.yaml. Wait: kubectl -n hune rollout status deployment/hune.

The edge routes the prefix of Hune to port 8080 of the Service hune. Prometheus reads /metrics on its port 9090; the edge never routes to this port. The CloudNativePG backups are the backup of the database. hune export (5.38), from a job of the operator, is the portable backup.

A failover of CloudNativePG lasts 1 to 2 minutes in this setup. During this time, reads come from the catalog and saves fail with 503; the editor keeps the draft (section 10).

17.9 Supply chain

  • Cargo.lock is committed. Every build and every test uses --locked.
  • Each direct dependency has an exact pin (=x.y.z), default-features = false, and a comment of one line with its reason.
  • procurauth-pep and procurauth-core are git dependencies of https://github.com/ProcurAuth/procurauth. Their rev is one full commit of 40 hex characters, and they have default-features = false. The job of 16.12 uses the same commit.
  • crates/hclconf/ is written in Hune and is not a copy. Its PROVENANCE file says so, gives the comparison with another HCL engine and records the edition.
  • web/js/vendor/chdash/ and web/css/vendor/chdash/ hold a copy of the ClickHouse Dash chart engine at a recorded commit, with the MIT notice. The lock file web/js/vendor/chdash/chdash.lock.json holds the SHA-256 of each file (9.24). tools/update-chdash.sh prints the upstream differences for a manual update. Nothing updates the copy automatically.
  • The repository commits one directory under vendor/: the local copy of mongodb (8b.2). The file vendor/mongodb.lock.json holds the checksum of the original crate and the SHA-256 of each file, and vendor/mongodb.patch holds the edits. After an edit, tools/check-vendor-lock.sh --mongodb-update writes both again, and the job policy checks them. The build of 17.2 makes target/cargo-vendor/ in stage 1 and builds offline in stage 2. Nothing goes to the upstream repository of the driver.
  • rust-toolchain.toml names one exact version. tests/images.lock pins every image by digest.

The MongoDB source uses two crates from crates.io:

  • mongodb =3.10.0 (Apache-2.0), the official driver, as a dependency of crates/hune-source behind the cargo feature mongo-driver. It has no default features. The features are compat-3-3-0, bson-3 and rustls-tls. The build uses the patched copy vendor/mongodb/ (8b.2), and deps.lock.txt lists it as mongodb 3.10.0 (vendor).
  • bson =3.1.0 (MIT), the BSON types of the driver. It reads no environment variable, so it is not a patched copy.
  • Two crates of the graph have a licence outside the allow list of deny.toml: webpki-roots (CDLA-Permissive-2.0) and tiny-keccak (CC0-1.0, a crate of the build). deny.toml gives each crate its own exception, and the list stays the same. webpki-roots is also a ban with the exception wrappers = ["mongodb"]: a test proves that the driver trusts no root other than tls.ca_file.

deny.toml:

toml
[graph]
targets = ["x86_64-unknown-linux-musl", "aarch64-unknown-linux-musl"]

[advisories]
version = 2
yanked = "deny"
ignore = [
  { id = "RUSTSEC-2026-0387", reason = "Hune only verifies ES256 signatures with public keys; remove when procurauth-core moves to p256 0.14" },
]

[licenses]
version = 2
allow = ["Apache-2.0", "MIT", "BSD-2-Clause", "BSD-3-Clause", "ISC", "Unicode-3.0", "Zlib"]
confidence-threshold = 0.93
exceptions = [
  { allow = ["CDLA-Permissive-2.0"], name = "webpki-roots" },
  { allow = ["CC0-1.0"], name = "tiny-keccak" },
]

[bans]
multiple-versions = "warn"
wildcards = "deny"
allow-wildcard-paths = true
deny = [
  { name = "openssl" }, { name = "openssl-sys" }, { name = "native-tls" },
  { name = "aws-lc-rs" }, { name = "aws-lc-sys" },
  { name = "reqwest" }, { name = "sqlx" }, { name = "axum" },
  { name = "rustls-native-certs" }, { name = "rustls-platform-verifier" },
  { name = "webpki-roots", wrappers = ["mongodb"] },
  { name = "hickory-resolver" }, { name = "hickory-proto" }, { name = "aws-config" }, { name = "aws-sigv4" },
]

[sources]
unknown-registry = "deny"
unknown-git = "deny"
allow-registry = ["https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index"]
allow-git = ["https://github.com/ProcurAuth/procurauth"]

The bans hold these rules:

  • one TLS stack, rustls with ring;
  • no HTTP client that reads proxy variables;
  • no CA store of the system and no bundled roots;
  • no axum and no sqlx;
  • no SRV resolver and no AWS authentication for the MongoDB driver (8b.4).

allow-wildcard-paths = true accepts a dependency with no version from a crate of the workspace to another crate of the workspace or to a ProcurAuth crate. Such a dependency has a path or a pinned git commit. Each crate of the workspace has publish = false. A registry dependency with the version * still fails.

The list ignore of [advisories] has one advisory, which the project accepted on 2026-11-22. RUSTSEC-2026-0387 is in crypto-bigint 0.5, through p256 0.13 of procurauth-core. Hune only verifies ES256 signatures with public keys, and only the tests sign. Remove the entry when procurauth-core moves to p256 0.14.

A new licence, an exception or an ignored advisory needs a change of deny.toml in a reviewed commit.

deps.lock.txt holds one line <name> <version> for each crate of the resolved graph of the two targets, outside the workspace, sorted in byte order (LC_ALL=C). The policy job compares it with this output:

sh
for t in x86_64-unknown-linux-musl aarch64-unknown-linux-musl; do
  cargo metadata --locked --format-version 1 --filter-platform "$t" \
    | jq -r '(.resolve.nodes | map(.id)) as $ids
             | .packages[] | select(.id as $i | $ids | index($i)) | select(.source != null)
             | "\(.name) \(.version)"'
done | LC_ALL=C sort -u

The script tests/ci/policy.sh also lists the packages whose manifest is under vendor/, with the tag (vendor) after the version. The line mongodb 3.10.0 (vendor) shows that the crate is a local copy. The command tests/ci/policy.sh --print-deps prints the list. The byte order makes the list equal on the host and in the builder container. The rule wrappers = ["mongodb"] of deny.toml names the package, so it works for the copy.

So each new crate, and each new version, is a visible line in the diff of a commit. The reviewer checks it.

17.10 Versioning and the changelog

Hune uses Semantic Versioning 2.0.0. The first release is 0.1.0. The release 1.0.0 comes after the exit criteria of M7 (section 18).

The contract of a version has six parts. They are the command line, the configuration keys, the document format, the store schema, the metric names and the log events. The HTTP API is not in the contract, because the front end ships in the same binary (section 6).

ChangeBefore 1.0.0From 1.0.0
A key, a subcommand, a metric or an event is removed or renamedminormajor
A new format_version (stop, migrate-documents, start)minormajor
A store migration that an old binary cannot readminormajor
A new key, subcommand, metric, event or additive store migrationminorminor
A fix with no change of the contractpatchpatch

CHANGELOG.md uses ASD-STE100. Its rules:

  • The file starts with the section ## Unreleased. Then each version follows, newest first, as ## <version> - <YYYY-MM-DD>.
  • A section has the groups Added, Changed, Removed, Fixed and Security, in this order. An empty group is left out. A release section can end with the group Known limits.
  • A commit can change the contract, the behaviour, a dependency or a deployment file. Such a commit adds its line under ## Unreleased. The reviewer refuses a change without its line.
  • The line of a change that breaks the contract starts with BREAKING:. It names the update step of 17.11 that it needs.
  • The release commit renames ## Unreleased to the version and the date, and adds a new empty ## Unreleased. The release notes are that section (17.3).

17.11 Update procedure

Read the section of CHANGELOG.md for each version between the current and the new version. A BREAKING: line names a step below.

The runbooks of 14.18 to 14.20 give the operation steps. This paragraph gives the delivery view.

Volume, compose:

  1. Make a backup: hune export -c config.hcl --to <dir> (5.38), or copy the dashboard folder.
  2. Build the new image from the new release archive (17.5).
  3. When the new version has a new format_version, stop Hune. Run hune migrate-documents -c config.hcl with the new image, read its dry run, then run it with --write (4.42, 14.20).
  4. Change the image tag in the compose file. Run docker compose up -d --no-deps hune.
  5. Check: /readyz gives 200, hune version prints the new version, and the start log holds the configuration.

PostgreSQL, compose or Kubernetes:

  1. Make a backup: hune export to a directory, and check the last backup of CloudNativePG.
  2. Build and import the new image (17.8).
  3. When the new version has a new store schema, scale the Deployment to 0 (or stop the compose instances). Then run hune migrate-store with the new image and migrate.hcl, as the Job of 17.8 (14.19). An old binary refuses the new schema. Then give to the application role the grants that the CHANGELOG.md names for this migration (5.25).
  4. When the new version has a new format_version, scale the Deployment to 0 and run hune migrate-documents as in the volume case (4.42).
  5. Set the new image in the compose file or the Deployment, and apply it. Check as in step 5 above, on each instance.

No step migrates down. A return to the old version after a migration of the store or of the documents needs a new store. Start the old version on a new empty store, and run hune import of the backup of step 1. Without a migration, the old image tag is enough.
